\mathsf{S}^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{S}),\mathsf{T}^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{T})

e^{\prime\prime}
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime\prime})\in\mathsf{S}^{\prime}
and
(e^{\prime\prime},e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{T}^{\prime}
iff there exists relation
\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{S};\mathsf{T})
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}
. Finally, by
Equation

44
, we conclude that there exists relation
\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{S};\mathsf{T})
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}
if and only if there exists
\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{R})
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}
.

•

If
\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{S}^{+}
,
(e,e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{R}
if and only if there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{S}^{k}
. By the previous point, there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{S}^{k}
if and only if there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and relation
\mathsf{S}^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{S})^{k}
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{S}^{\prime}
. Finally, by
Equation

44
, we conclude that there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and relation
\mathsf{S}^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{S})^{k}
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in\sigma(\mathsf{S})^{k}
if and only if there exists relation
\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{R})
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}
.

∎

Obtaining a consistency model in simple form from a consistency model in almost simple form is straightforward: every visibility formula is transformed by splitting composed relations into simpler subrelations and omitting
\mathtt{id}
by merging two existentially quantified events.
Lemma

B.4
formally describes such procedure.

See
B.4

Proof.

We construct a consistency model,
\mathsf{simple}(\mathsf{CMod})
, that is in simple form and it is equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
. The model is formally defined as follows:

(45)

\mathsf{simple}(\mathsf{CMod})=\{\mathsf{simple}(a)\ |\ a\in\mathsf{almost}(\mathsf{CMod})\}

where
\mathsf{simple}(a)
is the
simple visibility formula
of
a
.

The simple visibility formula of a visibility formula in almost form
a
is the visibility formula
f
obtained by supressing
\mathtt{id}
and compositional operators. Formally,
f
is the visibility formula s.t. (1)
\mathsf{len}(f)=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{len}(a)}\mathsf{count}(\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{a})
and (2) for every
i,1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(f)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{f}=\mathsf{rel}(\mathsf{Rel}_{j}^{a},i-k_{j})
; where
j
is the maximum index s.t.
k_{j}<i
and
k_{j}=\sum_{l=1}^{j}\mathsf{count}(\mathsf{Rel}_{l}^{a})
, and
\mathsf{count}
and
\mathsf{rel}
are the functions described in
Equation

46
and
Equation

47
respectively.

The function
\mathsf{count}
counts the number of additional quantifiers the correspondant simple form requires:

(46)

\mathsf{count}(\mathsf{R})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\text{if }\mathsf{R}=\mathtt{id}\\
1&\text{if }\mathsf{R}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\text{ or }{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}\\
\mathsf{count}(\mathsf{S})+\mathsf{count}(\mathsf{T})&\text{if }\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{S};\mathsf{T}\\
\end{array}\right.

Also, the function
\mathsf{rel}
, given a relation using compositional operator and an index
i
, returns the
i
-th component:

(47)

\mathsf{rel}(\mathsf{R},i)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{R}&\text{if }\mathsf{R}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\text{ or }{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}\\
\mathsf{rel}(\mathsf{S},i)&\text{if }i\leq\mathsf{count}(\mathsf{S})\\
\mathsf{rel}(\mathsf{T},i-\mathsf{count}(\mathsf{S}))&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

By construction,
\mathsf{simple}(\mathsf{CMod})
is in simple form. Clearly,
\mathsf{simple}(\mathsf{CMod})
is equivalent to
\mathsf{almost}(\mathsf{CMod})
. Then, thanks to
Proposition

B.5
, we conclude that
\mathsf{simple}(\mathsf{CMod})
is equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
.
∎

Removing Vacuous Visibility Formulas

After proving the existence of a consistency model
\mathsf{CMod}
in simple form equivalent to a given one, we show how to transform it for obtaining an equivalent consistency model
\mathsf{CMod}
without vacuous visibility formulas (
Lemma

B.7
). We say that any such consistency model is in
basic normal form
, extending
Definition

6.1
to any consistency model whose visibility formulas are described using
Equation

7
.

The following result, key to prove
Lemma

B.7
, it is a simple consequence of
Definition

B.2
and
Lemma

B.3
.

Proposition B.6.

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}
be a maximally-layered operation specification, and let
\mathsf{CMod}_{1},\mathsf{CMod}_{2}
be two consistency models s.t.
\mathsf{CMod}_{1}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}_{2}
but
\mathsf{CMod}_{1}\preccurlyeq\mathsf{CMod}_{2}
. There exists an abstract execution
\xi
valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}_{1}
, an object
x
and events
w,r
s.t.
w\in\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{2}])\setminus\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{1}])
.

Proof.

First of all, as
\mathsf{CMod}_{1}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}_{2}
but
\mathsf{CMod}_{1}\preccurlyeq\mathsf{CMod}_{2}
, by
Lemma

7.11
, there exists an abstract execution
\xi
valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}_{1}
, an object
x
and an event
r
s.t.
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{2}])\neq\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{1}])
. Thus, either
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{2}])\setminus\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{1}])\neq\emptyset
or
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{1}])\setminus\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{2}])\neq\emptyset
.

On one hand, if
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{2}])\setminus\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{1}])\neq\emptyset
, by Property
1
of
Definition

B.2
, then
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{2}])\setminus\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{1}])\neq\emptyset
. On the other hand, if
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{1}])\setminus\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{2}])\neq\emptyset
, by Property
2
of
Definition

B.2
,
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{2}])\setminus\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{1}])\neq\emptyset
.
∎

Lemma B.7.

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}
be an operation specification. For every consistency model
\mathsf{CMod}
in simple form, there exists a
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent consistency model,
\mathsf{bn}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
, that is in basic normal form w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
.

Proof.

To prove the result, we construct a consistency model in basic normal form w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
,
\mathsf{bn}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
, that is
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
. Without loss of generality we can assume that
\mathsf{CMod}
is ordered. Let
\alpha
be an ordinal of cardinality
|\mathsf{CMod}|
. We denote by
v^{i},0\leq i<\alpha
to the
i
-th visibility formula in
\mathsf{CMod}
7
7

7

Without loss of generality, we can assume that limit ordinals in
\alpha
are not associated to a visibility formula.
.

We construct a sequence of nested consistency models
\mathsf{CMod}_{k},0\leq k\leq\alpha
s.t. (1)
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
is
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
, (2)
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
is more succinct than
\mathsf{CMod}_{i}
(i.e., for every
i<k
,
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
iff
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{i}
and for every
i>k
,
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
), and (3) the first
k
visibility formulas of
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
are simple and non-vacuous w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{k},\mathsf{OpSpec})
(i.e., for every
i,0\leq i<k
, if
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
, then
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}\setminus\{v^{i}\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
).

We construct such sequence using transfinite induction. The base case,
k=0
, corresponds to
\mathsf{CMod}_{0}=\mathsf{CMod}
, which trivially satisfies (1), (2) and (3). For the successor case, let us assume that the property holds for the consistency model
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
, and let us prove it for
\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}
. If
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}\setminus\{v^{k}\}\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
, we denote
\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}
as
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}\setminus\{v^{k}\}
; and otherwise,
\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}=\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
.

Clearly, by construction of
\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}
, (1) and (2) immediately hold. For proving (3), we observe that if
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}
,
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{i}
. In such case,
\mathsf{CMod}_{i}\setminus\{v^{i}\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
. Hence, by
Lemma

7.11
, there exists an abstract execution valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{i}\setminus\{v^{i}\},\mathsf{OpSpec})
that is not valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. As
\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}\subseteq\mathsf{CMod}_{i}
,
\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}\setminus\{v^{i}\}\subseteq\mathsf{CMod}_{i}\setminus\{v^{i}\}
and hence,
\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}\setminus\{v^{i}\}\preccurlyeq\mathsf{CMod}_{i}\setminus\{v^{i}\}
. Therefore, by
Lemma

7.11
,
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}\setminus\{v^{i}\},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Thus, as
\xi
is not valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
,
\mathsf{CMod}_{k+1}\setminus\{v^{i}\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
; so we conclude (3).

For the limit case, we define
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
as the intersection of all consistency models
\mathsf{CMod}_{i},i<k
. We observe that in this case, (2) immediately holds by construction of
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
.

For proving (3) we observe that
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
iff
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{i}
. In such case,
\mathsf{CMod}_{i}\setminus\{v^{i}\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
; so by
Lemma

7.11
, there exists an abstract execution
\xi
valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{i}\setminus\{v^{i}\},\mathsf{OpSpec})
that is not valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Similarly to the inductive case, we deduce using
Lemma

7.11
that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{k}\setminus\{v^{i}\},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Therefore, we conclude that
\mathsf{CMod}_{i}\setminus\{v^{i}\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
.

For proving (1), we reason by contradiction, assuming that
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
and reaching a contradiction. In such case, by
Lemma

7.11
there exists an abstract execution
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{k},\mathsf{OpSpec})
that is not valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.
W.l.o.g., we can assume that
\xi
is minimal w.r.t. the number of events in it; and let
\mathsf{len}(\xi)
the number of events in such execution.

For each event
r\in\xi
, we define an ordinal
i(r),i(r)<k
associated to every visibility formula
v^{i},i<k
that can be applied on
\xi
. First, we note that for every pair of events,
e,e^{\prime}
and object
x
, if a visibility formula
v_{x}(e,e^{\prime})
holds in
\xi
,
\mathsf{len}(v)\leq\mathsf{len}(\xi)
. Observe that there exists finite number of visibility formulas
v
in
\mathsf{CMod}
with at most length
\mathsf{len}(\xi)
: on one hand, for each
j,1\leq j\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{j}^{v}
is either
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. On the other hand,
\mathsf{wrCons}
is defined as a conjunction of predicates from a finite set. Thus, the number of possible visibility formulas
v
of length
\mathsf{len}(v)\leq\mathsf{len}(\xi)
is finite. Let
i_{r}
be the biggest index of a visibility formula
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}
s.t.
\mathsf{len}(v^{i})\leq\mathsf{len}(\xi)
and
i<k
; and let
i(r)=i_{r}+1
. Observe that
k
is a limit ordinal,
i(r)<k
.

Let
x
be an object and
r
be an event in
\xi
. We show that
\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{k}])=\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(r)}])
. As
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}\subseteq\mathsf{CMod}_{i(r)}
,
\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{k}])\subseteq\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(r)}])
. For showing
\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(r)}])\subseteq\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{k}])
, let
w\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(r)}])
. In such case, there exists a visibility formula
v^{i}
s.t.
v^{i}(w,r)
holds in
\xi
. If
i>k
, by (2)
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
. Otherwise,
i<i(r)
, so by (2),
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{i}
. Observe that in this case, applying the induction hypothesis (2) on every consistency model
\mathsf{CMod}_{j},j<k
,
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{j}
, we deduce that
v^{i}\in\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
. Either way, we deduce that
w\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{k}])
. In conclusion,
\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{k}])=\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(r)}])
.

We conclude a contradiction by showing that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
; which by assumption it is not. Let
e
be the last event w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
in
\xi
. For reaching such contradiction, as
i(e)<k
and
\mathsf{CMod}_{i(e)}\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
, it suffices to show that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{i(e)},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. We show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(e^{\prime})=\mathsf{rspec}(e^{\prime})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(e)}])
.

On one hand, if
e^{\prime}=e
, we note that
\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(e,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{k}])=\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(e,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(e)}])
. As
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{k},\mathsf{OpSpec})
, we conclude that
\mathsf{rspec}(e)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(e)}])={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(e)
.

On the other hand, if
e^{\prime}\neq e
, let
\xi^{\prime}
be the execution obtained by removing
e
from
\xi
. By the minimality of
\xi
,
\xi^{\prime}
is valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. By induction hypothesis (1),
\mathsf{CMod}\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}_{i(e)}
. Hence,
\xi^{\prime}
is valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{i(e)},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. We thus deduce that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(e^{\prime})=\mathsf{rspec}(e^{\prime})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}_{i(e)}])
. In conclusion,
\mathsf{CMod}_{k}
satisfies (1) and thus, the inductive step.

Finally, we define
\mathsf{bn}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}=\mathsf{CMod}_{\alpha}
. As
\mathsf{CMod}_{\alpha}
satisfies (1) and (3), it is a consistency model
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
composed of finite, non-vacuous w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}_{\alpha},\mathsf{OpSpec})
visibility formulas; so we conclude that it is a consistency model in basic normal form.

∎

Conflict-Strengthening a Consistency Model

Lemma B.8.

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}
be an operation specification. For every consistency model
\mathsf{CMod}
in basic normal form w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
there exists a
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent consistency model that is in normal form.

Proof.

We transform
\mathsf{CMod}
to define
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
, a consistency model in normal form that is
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
.

For every visibility formula
v\in\mathsf{CMod}
, we define
v^{\prime}
as the visibility formula that only differs with
v
on its conflict predicate. More specifically, we require that for every set
E\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon_{0},\ldots\varepsilon_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
, we require that
\mathsf{conflict}(E)\in v^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathsf{conflict}_{x}(E)\in v^{\prime}
) iff (1) for every abstract execution
\xi
, every object
x
and every collection of events
e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
s.t.
v_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\xi_{v}
, there exists an object
y\neq x
s.t. if
\varepsilon_{i}\in E,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
, then
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i})(y,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])\downarrow
(resp.
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])\downarrow
) and (2) there is no strict superset of
E
satisfying (1). We define
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
as the set containing all such visibility formulas.
For conclude the result, we first prove that
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
for then deduce that
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
is indeed a consistency model in normal form.

We show that
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
. On one hand, as every visibility formula
v^{\prime}
enforces more conflicts than
v
,
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\preccurlyeq\mathsf{CMod}
. On the other hand, by the definition of
v^{\prime}
, for every abstract execution
\xi
, object
x
and events
w,r
, if
v^{\prime}_{x}(w,r)
holds in
\xi
,
v_{x}(w,r)
also holds in
\xi
. Altogether, we conclude that
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
.

To show that
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
is a consistency model in normal form, we observe that by construction, every visibility formula
v\in\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
is in simple form and it is conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
. Hence, it suffices to prove that every visibility formula
v\in\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
is non-vacuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
.

Let
v^{\prime}
be a visibility formula of
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
. Observe that by construction of
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
,
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\setminus\{v^{\prime}\}\equiv\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}
. Hence, as
\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
, we deduce that
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\setminus\{v^{\prime}\}\equiv\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}\equiv\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
. In other words,
v^{\prime}
is non-vacuous w.r.t.
(\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

∎

B.2.
Arbitration-Free Well-Formedness

As described in
Section

7.1
, a consistency model is arbitration-free if a
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent consistency model in normal form is arbitration-free. In
Theorem

B.9
, we present a result that states that arbitration-free is well-defined, as either every
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent consistency model in normal form are arbitration-free or none.

Regarding notations, for a visibility formula
v
and
i,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
we denote hereinafter
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon_{0},\ldots\varepsilon_{\mathsf{len}(v)}))
to the sets of conflicts of
\varepsilon_{i}
in
v
, i.e.
E\in\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}
iff
\varepsilon_{i}\in E
and
\mathsf{conflict}(E)\in v
.

Theorem B.9.

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}=(E,\mathsf{rspec},\mathsf{extract},\mathsf{wspec})
be an operation specification and let
\mathsf{CMod}
be a consistency model. For every pair of consistency models in normal form
n_{1},n_{2}
that are
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
,
n_{1}
is arbitration-free iff
n_{2}
is arbitration-free.

Proof.

We prove the result by contradiction, assuming that there exists two consistency models
n_{1},n_{2}
in normal form,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
, but one of them arbitration-free and the other one no. W.l.o.g., we can assume that
n_{1}
is arbitration-free and
n_{2}
is not. On one hand, as
n_{2}
is not arbitration-free w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, there exists a visibility formula
v\in n_{2}
s.t.
v
is not arbitration-free. We construct an abstract execution that is valid w.r.t.
(n_{1},\mathsf{OpSpec})
but not valid w.r.t.
(n_{2},\mathsf{OpSpec})
using
v
, reaching a contradiction.

First of all, observe that by
Lemma

6.4
,
n_{1}
is weaker than
CC
. The abstract execution we construct contains a collection events
e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
s.t.
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
and
v_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds on it; for some object
x
.

Let
x
be an object. For each set
E\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
we consider a distinct object
y_{E}
, also distinct from
x
. These objects represents each different conflict in
v
in an explicit manner.

We denote by
E_{x}\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
to the set s.t.
\mathsf{conflict}_{x}(E_{x})\in v
. Also, for every
i,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
, we denote by
X_{i}
to the set containing objects
y_{E}
(resp.
x
) iff
E\in\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}
(resp.
E_{x}\in\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}
). We denote by
X
to the union of sets
X_{i},0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
.

For obtaining
\xi
, we construct a sequence of executions
\xi^{i},0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
inductively, starting from an initial event
init
, and incorporating at each time a new event
e_{i}
. We use the notation
h^{-1}
and
\xi^{-1}
to describe the history and abstract execution containing only
init
respectively. We use the convention
e_{-1}=\textup{{{init}}}
,
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,-1)}=\mathsf{Objs}
and
\tilde{x}_{-1}=o_{-1}=x
(the usage of such conventions will be clearer later).

For the inductive step, we assume that the abstract execution
\xi^{i-1}={({h^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{-1}})}
associated to the history
h^{i-1}={({E^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i-1}})}
contains events
e_{-1}\ldots e_{i-1}
and is well-defined (satisfies
Definition

3.4
) and we construct the history
h^{i}
and the abstract execution
\xi^{i}
. First of all, we impose the constraint that if
i>0
, then
r_{i}=r_{i-1}
iff
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
, and otherwise
r_{i}\neq r_{j},0\leq j<i
.

Also, we define a pair of special objects,
\tilde{x}_{i}
and
o_{i}
. The purpose of object
\tilde{x}_{i}
is control the number of events in
\xi
that write object
x
.
Equation

48
describes
\tilde{x}_{i}
; where
\mathsf{choice}
is a function that deterministically chooses an element from a non-empty set. The object
o_{i}
is an object different from objects
x,y_{E},E\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon_{0},\ldots\varepsilon_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
and
o_{j},-1\leq j<i
that we use for ensuring that if
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, then
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
.

(48)

\tilde{x}_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tilde{x}_{i-1}&\text{if }X_{i}=\emptyset\\
x&\text{if }X_{i}\neq\emptyset\text{ and }x\in X_{i}\\
\mathsf{choice}\left(X_{i}\right)&\text{if }X_{i}\neq\emptyset\text{ and }x\not\in X_{i}\end{array}\right.

We select a domain
D_{i}
, a set of objects
W_{i},W_{i}\subseteq D_{i}
that event
e_{i}
must write, and a set of objects
C_{i}\subseteq D_{i}
whose value needs to be corrected for
e_{i}
in
\xi_{i+1}
– in the sense of
Definition

7.13
. We distinguishing between several cases:

•

i=0
or
0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
: In this case, we select
e_{i}
to be a write event. If
\mathsf{OpSpec}
only allows single-object atomic read-write events, we define
D_{i}=X_{i}
; while if not, we consider a domain containing
o_{i-1},o_{i}
, every object in
X_{i}
but no object from
X\setminus X_{i}
nor objects
o_{j},0\leq j<\mathsf{len}(v),j\neq i-1,i
. Observe that by
Proposition

B.10
, such domain always exist on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
.

If there is an unconditional write event whose domain is
D_{i}
, we define
W_{i}=D_{i}
. Otherwise, we define
W_{i}=X_{i}\cup\{o_{i}\}
.

•

0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
: In this case, by
Proposition

B.11
,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows atomic read-write events. If
\mathsf{OpSpec}
only allows single-object atomic read-write events, we define
D_{i}=X_{i}
; while if not, we consider a domain containing
o_{i-1},o_{i}
, every object in
X_{i}
but no object from
X\setminus X_{i}
nor objects
o_{j},0\leq j<\mathsf{len}(v),j\neq i-1,i
. Observe that by
Proposition

B.10
, such domain always exist on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
.

Similarly to the previous case, if there is an unconditional atomic read-write event whose domain is
D_{i}
, we define
W_{i}=D_{i}
. Otherwise, we define
W_{i}=X_{i}\cup\{o_{i}\}
.

•

0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
and
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}=\emptyset
: In this case, by
Proposition

B.11
,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows events that do not unconditionally write. If
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows read events that are not write events, we select
D_{i}
to be the domain of any such event and
W_{i}=\emptyset
. Otherwise,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
must allow conditional write events; so we select
D_{i}
to be the domain of any such event,
W_{i}=\emptyset
. Observe that in this case, thanks to the assumptions on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
(see
Section

7.4
), we can assume without loss of generality that whenever
o_{i-1}\in D_{i-1}
,
o_{i-1}\in D_{i}
as well; while otherwise, that
\tilde{x}_{i-1}\in D_{i}
.

Finally we describe the event
e_{i}
thanks to the sets
D_{i}
and
W_{i}
. If
W_{i}=D_{i}
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, we select an unconditional atomic read-write event whose domain is
D_{i}
. If
W_{i}=D_{i}
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, we select an unconditional write event whose domain is
D_{i}
. If
W_{i}=\emptyset
and
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows read events that are not write events, we select a read event whose domain is
D_{i}
. Finally, if that is not the case, we select a conditional write event
e_{i}
s.t.
\mathsf{obj}({e_{i}})=D_{i}
and s.t. an execution-corrector exists for
(e_{i},W_{i},\tilde{x}_{i},\xi^{i-1}\oplus e_{i})
. Such event always exists by the assumptions on operation specifications (
Section

7.4
). W.l.o.g. we can assume that
e_{i}
happens on replica
r_{i}
.

For concluding the description of
h^{i}={({E_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}})}
and
\xi^{i}={({h^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}})}
, we use an auxiliary history and abstract execution,
h^{i}_{0}={({E^{i}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{0}})}
and
\xi^{i}_{0}={({h^{i}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}_{0}})}
respectively. For describing the write-read dependencies of
e_{i}
in
\xi_{i}^{0}
, we define the context mapping
c^{i}:\mathsf{Objs}\to\mathsf{Contexts}
, associating each object
y
to the context
c^{i}(y)
described in
Equation

49
.

(49)

c^{i}(y)=(F^{i}(y),{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1}_{\restriction F^{i}(y)\times F^{i}(y)},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i-1}_{\restriction F^{i}(y)\times F^{i}(y)})

where
F^{i}(y)
is the mapping associating each object
y
with the set of events described below:

F^{i}(y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{\textup{{{init}}}\}&\text{if }i=0\text{ or if }0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)\land\mathsf{Rel}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}\\
\left\{e\in E^{i-1}\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{wspec}(e)(y,[\xi^{i-1},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow\text{and}\\
\ (e,e_{i-1})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1})^{*}\end{array}\right.\right\}&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{array}\right.

Then, we define
\xi^{i}_{0}
as the abstract execution of the history
h^{i}_{0}={({E^{i}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{0}})}
obtained by appending
e_{i}
to
h^{i}_{0}
and
\xi^{i}_{0}
as follows:
E^{i}_{0}
contains
E^{i-1}
and event
e_{i}
. First of all, we require that the relations
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{0}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{0}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}_{0}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}_{0}
contain
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i-1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i-1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i-1}
respectively. With respect to event
e_{i}
, we impose that
e_{i}
is the maximal event w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{0}
among those on the same replica. Also,
e_{i}
is maximal w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
as we define that for every object
z
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{0}}_{z}^{-1}(e_{i})=\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i})(z,c_{i}(z))
. For describing
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}_{0}
, we require that for every event
e
s.t.
(e,e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{0}
,
(e,e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
. Also, if
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
, we impose that
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}_{0}
. Finally, we require that for every pair of events
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{i-1}
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1}
and
(e^{\prime},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{0}
,
(e,e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}_{0}
. With respect to
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}_{0}
, we impose that
e_{i}
is the maximum event w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
in
\xi^{i}_{0}
.

We use
\xi^{i}_{0}
to construct
\xi^{i}
. If event
e_{i}
is not a conditional write event,
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{0}
. Otherwise, if event
e_{i}
is a conditional write event, given
W_{i}
and object
\tilde{x}_{i}
, we select an execution-corrector for
e_{i}
w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
and
a_{i}
. W.l.o.g., we assume that every event mapped by
a_{i}
happens on replica
r_{i}
. Observe that by the choice of sets
D_{i}
and
W_{i}
, and thanks to the assumptions on storages (see
Section

7.4
), such event(s) are always well-defined.

In addition, we denote by
C_{i}
to the set of objects we need to correct for
e_{i}
. More specifically, if
e_{i}
is a conditional write-read, we denote by
C_{i}
to the set of objects
y
s.t.
a_{i}(y)
is defined, i.e.
C_{i}=\{y\in\mathsf{Objs}\ |\ a_{i}(y)\downarrow\}
. In the case
e_{i}
is not a conditional write-read, we use the convention
C_{i}=\emptyset
. The set of events in
\xi^{i}
is the following:
E^{i}=E^{i-1}\cup\{e_{i}\}\bigcup_{y\in C_{i}\setminus\{o_{i-1}\}}a_{i}(y)
. Observe that by the choice of
C_{i}
, the set
E^{i}
is well-defined.

Concerning notations, we use
c\oplus a
to denote the context obtained by appending
a
to the context
c=\{E,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}\}
as the
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
-maximum and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
-maximum event.

From
\xi^{i}_{0}
, we define
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{0}\overset{\mathsf{seq}(a_{i})}{\curlyvee}e_{i}
as the corrected execution of
\xi
and
e_{i}
with events
a_{i}
. For describing
\xi^{i}
, we consider
<
to be a well-founded order over
\mathsf{Objs}
.
\xi^{i}
satisfies the following:

•

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
: Let
y\in C_{i}
. We require that for every event
e\in E^{i-1}
,
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
iff
\mathtt{rep}(e)=r_{i},0\leq j<i
. We also require that
(\textup{{{init}}},a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
and
(a_{i}(y),e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
. Finally, we require that for every objects
y^{\prime}\in C_{i},y^{\prime}<y
,
(a_{i}(y^{\prime}),a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}
: Let
y
be an object in
C_{i}
. For every object
z
, if
z\in C_{i}
and
z<y
, we require that
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{z})^{-1}(a_{i}(y))=\mathsf{rspec}(a_{i}(y))(z,c^{i}(z)\oplus a_{i}(z))
; while otherwise, we require that
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{z})^{-1}(a_{i}(y))=\mathsf{rspec}(a_{i}(y))(z,c^{i}(z))
. We also require that for every object
z
, if
z\in C_{i}
, then
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{z})^{-1}(e_{i})=\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i})(z,c^{i}(z)\oplus a_{i}(z))
, while otherwise,
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{z})^{-1}(e_{i})=\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i})(z,c^{i}(z))
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
: Let
y\in C_{i}
. We require that for every object
y\in C_{i}
and event
e
s.t.
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}
,
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
. Also, if
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
, we impose that
(e_{i-1},a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
. Finally, we require that for every pair of events
e,e^{\prime}\in E^{i-1}
s.t.
(e,e^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1}
and
(e^{\prime},a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
,
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
: We impose that for every event
e\in E^{i-1}
,
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i},y\in C_{i}
. We also require that for every pair of objects
y_{1},y_{2}\in C_{i}
s.t.
y_{1},y_{2}
,
(a_{i}(y_{1}),a_{i}(y_{2}))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
.

We then define
h^{i}={({E^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}})}
and
\xi^{i}={({h^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}})}
. Observe that by construction of
h^{i}
and
\xi^{i}
, they satisfy
Definitions

3.2
and
3.4
respectively; so they are a history and an abstract execution respectively. In particular, observe that
\xi^{i}
is a correction of the abstract execution
\xi^{i-1}_{0}
with events
a_{i}
.

Finally, we define
h={({E,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
and
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
as, respectively, the history
h^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
and the abstract execution
\xi^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
. We prove that
\xi
is the abstract execution we were looking for.

First, we show that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
n_{2}
: as
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
(
Corollary

B.13
), so by
Lemma

6.4
, it is valid w.r.t.
(n_{1},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. As
n_{1}\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}n_{2}
,
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(n_{2},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Next, we deduce in
Proposition

B.16
that
\mathsf{OpSpec}
is maximally layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. For proving such result, we rely on
Propositions

B.14
and
B.15
. Finally, we conclude in
Proposition

B.17
that the layer bound of
\mathsf{rspec}
is bounded by the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
v
. However, this implies that
v
is vacuous w.r.t.
n_{2}
(
Proposition

B.18
); which is impossible by the choice of
v
. The contradiction arises from assuming that
n_{1}
is arbitration-free but
n_{2}
is not; so we conclude the result.
∎

Proposition B.10.

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}
be a storage that allows multi-object write (resp. read-write) events whose domain is not
\mathsf{Objs}
. Then, for every pair of finite disjoint sets
F_{1},F_{2}
there exists a domain
D
in
\mathsf{OpSpec}
s.t.
F_{1}\subseteq D
but
F_{2}\cap D=\emptyset
.

Proof.

The result is immediate as
F_{1}
is finite. Hence, by the assumptions on operation specifications (
Section

7.4
),
F_{1}
is a domain on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
.
∎

Proposition B.11.

Let
v
be a visibility formula and
i,0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
. If
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows read-write events. If
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}=\emptyset
allows events that do not unconditionally write.

Proof.

Observe that as
v
is non-vacuous w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
,
\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
. By
Proposition

B.6
, there exists an execution
\overline{\xi}
valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}
, an object
z
and events
f_{0},\ldots f_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
s.t.
v_{z}(f_{0},\ldots f_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\overline{\xi}
.

On one hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, as
\overline{\xi}
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}
, there exists
z
s.t.
\mathsf{rspec}(f_{i})(z,[\overline{\xi},\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}])\neq\emptyset
. Also,
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
iff
f_{i}
writes on some object
z^{\prime}
. Hence,
f_{i}
is a read-write event.

On the other hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}=\emptyset
, as
v
is conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, event
f_{i}
does not necessarily write any object. Thus,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows events that do not unconditionally write.
∎

Proposition B.12.

The abstract execution
\xi
described in
Theorem

B.9
satisfies that for every
i,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
:

(1)

For every object
y\in C_{i}
, the following conditions hold:

(a)

For every object
z\in\mathsf{Objs}
, if
z\in C_{i}
and
z<y
,
G(a_{i}(y),z)=F^{i}(z)\cup\{a_{i}(z)\}
, while otherwise,
G(a_{i}(y),z)=F^{i}(z)
.

(b)

The execution
\xi^{i}\restriction y
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

(2)

For the event
e_{i}
, the following conditions hold:

(a)

For every object
z
, if
z\in C_{i}
,
G(e_{i},z)=F^{i}(z)\cup\{a_{i}(z)\}
, while otherwise
G(e_{i},z)=F^{i}(z)
.

(b)

The execution
\xi^{i}
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

where
\mathsf{ctxt}_{z}(e,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])={({G(e,z),{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}_{\restriction G(e,z)\times G(e,z)},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{\restriction G(e,z)\times G(e,z)}})}
.

Proof.

We prove the result by induction. In particular, we show that for every
i,-1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
and object
y
, either (0)
i=-1
or (1) and (2) hold. The base case,
i=-1
, is immediate as (0) holds; so let us suppose that the result holds for every
j,-1\leq j<i
, and let us prove it for
i
.

For proving the inductive step, we first prove (1) and then (2). As both (1) and (2) have an identical proof (observe that the role of object
y
in the former is just to declare that event
a_{i}(y)
is well-defined), we present only the proof of (1).

We show (1) by transfinite induction. Let
\alpha
be an ordinal of cardinality
|\mathsf{Objs}|
. For every
k,0\leq k\leq\alpha
, we denote by
V_{k}
to the set containing the first
k
elements in
\mathsf{Objs}
according to
<
. We show that (1) holds for every
y\in V_{k}\cap C_{i}
.

The base,
V_{0}
is immediate as
V_{0}=\emptyset
. We thus focus on the successor case (i.e., showing that if (1) holds for every object
y\in V_{k}\cap C_{i}
it also holds for
V_{k+1}
), as the limit case is immediate: if
k
is a limit ordinal,
V_{k}=\bigcup_{i,i<k}V_{i}
; so (1) immediately holds. For showing that (1) holds for every object
y\in V_{k+1}\cap C_{i}
, as by induction hypothesis it holds for every object
y\in V_{k}\cap C_{i}
, it suffices to show it for the only object
y\in V_{k+1}\setminus V_{i}
. W.l.o.g., we can assume that
y\in C_{i}
; as otherwise the result is immediate.

We first prove (1a) and then we show (1b). Let
z\in\mathsf{Objs}
be an object. Two cases arise depending on
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
.

On one hand, if
i=0
or
i>0\land\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
,
F^{i}(z)=\{\textup{{{init}}}\}
. As
\textup{{{init}}}\in G(a_{i}(y),z)
, it suffices to show that the only non-initial event in
E
in
G(a_{i}(y),z)
is
a_{i}(z)
(whenever
z\in C_{i}
and
z<y
). Observe that an event
e
belongs to
G(a_{i}(y),z)
if
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
. As
a_{i}(y)\in E^{i}
, by construction of
\xi
,
e
must belong to
E^{i}
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,a_{i}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{+}
.

Observe that as either
i=0
or
0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)\land\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, by definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
,
e\not\in E^{i-1}
. Thus,
e
must be an event in
E^{i}\setminus E^{i-1}
. Observe that by construction of
\xi
, as
(e,a_{i}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{+}
, such event must be an event
a_{i}(w),w\in C_{i},w<y
. As
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{0}\overset{a_{i}}{\curlyvee}e_{i}
, by induction hypothesis (1b), we deduce that
\xi^{i}\restriction w
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Hence, as
\mathsf{wspec}(a_{i}(w))(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
, we deduce thanks to Property
1
of
Definition

7.13
that
z=w
– so
z\in C_{i}
and
z<y
.

On the other hand, if
0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)\land\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, two sub-cases arise:
z\in C_{i},z<y
or not. Both cases are identical, so we present the former, i.e., if
z\in C_{i},z<y
, then
F^{i}(z)\cup\{a_{i}(z)\}=G(a_{i}(y),z)
.

For proving that
F^{i}(z)\cup\{a_{i}(z)\}\subseteq G(a_{i}(y),z)
, we split the proof in two blocks: showing that
F^{i}(z)\subseteq G(a_{i}(y),z)
and showing that
a_{i}(z)\in G(a_{i}(y),z)
.

For showing that
F^{i}(z)\subseteq G(a_{i}(y),z)
, let
e
be an event in
F^{i}(z)
. In such case, to
e\in E^{i-1}
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,e_{i-1})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{*}
. By the construction of
\xi
, it is easy to see that any such event belongs to
E^{i}
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,e_{i-1})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{*}
. As
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, we deduce that
(e_{i-1},a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
. Hence,
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
; so
e\in G(a_{i}(y),z)
. This show that
F^{i}(z)\subseteq G(a_{i}(y),z)
.

For showing that
a_{i}(z)\in G(a_{i}(y),z)
, we observe that
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{0}\overset{a_{i}}{\curlyvee}e_{i}
. As
z<y
, by induction hypothesis (1b),
\xi^{i}\restriction z
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Thus, by Property
1
of
Definition

7.13
,
\mathsf{wspec}(a_{i}(z))(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
. Hence,
\mathsf{wspec}(a_{i}(z))(z,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
. As
z<y
,
(a_{i}(z),a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
; so we conclude that
a_{i}(z)\in G(a_{i}(y),z)
.

We conclude the proof of the inductive step of (1a) by showing the converse i.e.
F^{i}(z)\cup\{a_{i}(z)\}\supseteq G(a_{i}(y),z)
. Let
e\in G(a_{i}(y),z)
. First of all, by the definition of
Causal
visibility formula (see
Figure

4(b)
),
e\in G(a_{i}(y),z)
iff
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
. Observe that if
(e,a_{i}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
, by construction of
\xi
, such event must belong to
E^{i}
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,a_{i}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{+}
. We prove that if
e\in E^{i-1}
then
e\in F^{i}(z)
, while otherwise, if
e\in E^{i}\setminus E^{i-1}
, then
e=a_{i}(z)
.

If
e\in E^{i-1}
, as
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i-1},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
. Also, as
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
and
(e,a_{i}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{+}
, we deduce that
(e,e_{i-1})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1})^{*}
. In other words,
e\in F^{i}(z)
.

Otherwise, if
e\in E^{i}\setminus E^{i-1}
, we note that by construction of
\xi
, the only events in
E^{i}\setminus E^{i-1}
s.t.
(e,a_{i}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{+}
are events
a_{i}(w),w\in C_{i},w<y
. As
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{0}\overset{\mathsf{seq}(a_{i})}{\curlyvee}e_{i}
and
z<y
,
\xi^{i}\restriction z
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Thus, as
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
, by Property
1
of
Definition

7.13
we conclude that
e=a_{i}(z)
.

For concluding the inductive step, we show that (1b) holds. This is immediate by the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}
: for every event
e\in\xi^{i}\restriction y
, by induction hypothesis (1a) or (2a) – depending on whether
e=e_{j}
or
a_{j}(w)
, where
0\leq j\leq i,w\in C_{i}
–
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i})_{z}^{-1}(e)=\mathsf{rspec}(e)(\textup{{CC}},[\xi^{i}\restriction y,z])
. Thus,
\xi^{i}\restriction y
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

∎

A consequence of
Proposition

B.12
is the following result.

Corollary B.13.

The abstract execution
\xi
described in
Theorem

B.9
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

Proposition B.14.

The predicate
v_{x_{0}}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in the abstract execution
\xi
described in
Theorem

B.9
.

Proof.

The proof is a simple consequence of
\xi
’s construction. To show that
v_{x_{0}}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\xi
, we first show that for every
i,1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
and to then prove that
\mathsf{wrCons}^{\mathsf{v}}_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\xi
.

We prove that for every
i,1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
. Four cases arise depending on
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
.

•

\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
: In this case, by construction of events
e_{i-1},e_{i}
, we know that
r_{i}=r_{i-1}
. Hence,
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
.

•

\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
: In this case, we first show that there is an object
y\in D_{i}\cap W_{i-1}\setminus C_{i}
, and then show that
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{y}
. For showing the first part, we distinguish between cases depending on whether
o_{i-1}\in D_{i}
or not.

–

o_{i-1}\in D_{i}
: In this sub-case, we show that
y=o_{i-1}
. On one hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}=\emptyset
, by the choice of event
e_{i}
,
o_{i-1}\in D_{i-1}\setminus C_{i}
. On the other hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
, as
o_{i-1}\in D_{i}
, we deduce that
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows multi-object read-write events. Observe that as
v
is conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
,
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i-1)}\neq\emptyset
. Hence, as
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows multi-object read-write events, we deduce that
o_{i-1}\in D_{i-1}\setminus C_{i}
. In both cases, as
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i-1)}\neq\emptyset
and
o_{i-1}\in D_{i-1}
, by the choice of
W_{i-1}
, we conclude that
o_{i-1}\in W_{i-1}
.

–

o_{i-1}\not\in D_{i}
: In this case, we show that
y=\tilde{x}_{i}
. On one hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}=\emptyset
,
X_{i}=\emptyset
; so by the choice of
\tilde{x}_{i}
(see
Equation

48
),
\tilde{x}_{i}=\tilde{x}_{i-1}
. By the choice of
D_{i}
,
\tilde{x}_{i-1}\in D_{i}\setminus C_{i}
. Moreover, as
v
is conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
,
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i-1)}\neq\emptyset
; so
\tilde{x}_{i-1}\in X_{i-1}
. By the choice of event
e_{i-1}
,
X_{i-1}\subseteq W_{i-1}
. Altogether, we conclude that
\tilde{x}_{i}\in W_{i-1}
.

On the other hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
, we note that
\tilde{x}_{i}\in D_{i}\setminus C_{i}
. As
o_{i-1}\not\in D_{i}
, we deduce that
\mathsf{OpSpec}
only allows single-object read-write events. Thus,
D_{i}=\{\tilde{x}_{i}\}
. As
v
is conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, we deduce that
X_{i}\subseteq X_{i-1}
. As by the choice of
e_{i-1}
,
X_{i-1}\subseteq W_{i-1}
, we conclude that
\tilde{x}_{i}\in W_{i-1}
.

We prove now that
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{y}
. First, we show that
{e_{i-1}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {y}\ \mathsf{in}\ {\xi}
. On one hand, if
e_{i-1}
is an unconditional write event,
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i-1})(y,c^{i}(y))\downarrow
. On the other hand, if
e_{i-1}
is a conditional write event, as
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
(
Corollary

B.13
) and
y\in W_{i}
, by Property
2
of
Definition

7.13
, we deduce that
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i-1})(y,c^{i}(y))\downarrow
. Then, as
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
,
e_{i-1}\in F^{i}(y)
. Observe that by construction of
\xi
,
e_{i-1}
is the
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
-maximum event in
c^{i}(y)
. We note that as
y\not\in C_{i}
, by
Proposition

B.12
,
F^{i}(y)=G(e_{i},y)
. To sum up,
e_{i-1}
is the
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
-maximum event in
\mathsf{ctxt}_{y}(e_{i},[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])
. As
\mathsf{rspec}
is maximally layered, we deduce that
e_{i-1}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i})(y,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])
. Finally, as
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
CC
(
Corollary

B.13
), we conclude that
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{y}
.

•

\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
: In this case, we explicitly stated that
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
during the construction of
\xi
.

•

\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
: Similarly, by definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
, we know that
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
.

For showing that show that
\mathsf{wrCons}^{\mathsf{v}}_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
, we show that for every
i,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
and every set
E\in\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}
, the event
e_{i}
writes on object
y_{E}
8
8

8

For simplifying the proof, we abuse of notation and say that
y_{E}=x
if
E=E_{x}
. Observe that
v
is conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, either
\mathsf{conflict}_{x}(E_{x})
or
\mathsf{conflict}(E_{x})
do not belong to
v
.
. If
e_{i}
is an unconditional write, by the choice of
e_{i}
, it writes on every object in
D_{i}
. As
y_{E}\in D_{i}
, we conclude that
e_{i}
writes on
y_{E}
. Otherwise, if
e_{i}
is a conditional write, we observe that
y_{E}\in W_{i}
. Hence, as
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{0}\overset{\mathsf{seq}(a_{i})}{\curlyvee}e_{i}
and
\xi^{i}
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
(
Proposition

B.12
), we deduce using Property
2
of
Definition

7.13
that
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i})(y_{E},[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
. By construction of
\xi
, we conclude that
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i})(y_{E},[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
.
∎

Proposition B.15.

Let
\xi
be the abstract execution described in
Theorem

B.9
. For every
i,0\leq i<\mathsf{len}(v)
, if the
\varepsilon_{i}
suffix of
v
is non-arbitration-free, then
(e_{i},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
.

Proof.

The proof is just an observation about the construction of
\xi
: for every
j,0<j\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
(e_{j-1},e_{j})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
iff
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. Hence,
(e_{i},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+})
iff for every
j,i<j\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{j}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. In particular, if the
\varepsilon_{i}
suffix of
v
is non-arbitration-free, then
(e_{i},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
.
∎

Proposition B.16.

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}
be a storage,
\mathsf{CMod}
be a consistency model in normal form w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
and
v
be a visibility formula in
\mathsf{CMod}
. If there exists an abstract execution
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
, an object
x
and events
w,r
s.t.
v_{x}(w,r)
holds in
\xi
but
(w,r)\not\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}})^{+}
, then
\mathsf{OpSpec}
is maximally layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
.

Proof.

First of all, as
v_{x}(w,r)
holds in
\xi
,
w\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
. If
\mathsf{OpSpec}
would be maximally layered w.r.t.
({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}})^{+}
,
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
contains at least the first layer of
\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
. Hence, there would exist an event
w^{\prime}
s.t.
w^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
and
(w,w^{\prime})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}})^{+}
. As
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
, we deduce that
(w^{\prime},r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
. By
Definition

3.4
, we deduce that
(w^{\prime},r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
. However, this implies that
(w,w^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
; which contradicts the assumptions. Hence,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
must be maximally layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
.
∎

Proposition B.17.

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}
be a storage maximally layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
,
\mathsf{CMod}
be a consistency model in normal form w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
and
v
be a non-arbitration free visibility formula in
\mathsf{CMod}
. Let us suppose that there exists an abstract execution
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
, an object
x
and events
e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
satisfying the following:

(1)

for every non-initial event
e
in
\xi
, if
e\not\in\{e_{i}\ |\ 0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)\}
, then
e
does not write on
x
in
\xi
,

(2)

v_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\xi
, and

(3)

for every non-arbitration-free
\varepsilon_{k}
-suffix of
v
,
(e_{k},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
.

In such case, the layer bound of
\mathsf{OpSpec}
is bounded by the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
v
.

Proof.

We reason by contradiction, assuming that
k
is bigger than the number of saturable suffixes of
v
. We first show that
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
contains less than
k
events in
\{e_{i}\ |\ 0\leq i<\mathsf{len}(v)\}
, for then deduce that
\textup{{{init}}}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
. After that, we reach a contradiction by showing that
e_{0}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
but
(e_{0},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
; which contradicts that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
.

We first show that
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
contains less than
k
events in
\{e_{i}\ |\ 0\leq i<\mathsf{len}(v)\}
. As
v
contains less than
k
saturable suffixes, by the Assumption
3
, there is less than
k
events in
\{e_{i}\ |\ 0\leq i<\mathsf{len}(v)\}
that write on
x
in
\xi
and that succeed
e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
. As
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
(see
Definition

3.4
), we deduce that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
contains less than
k
events in
\{e_{i}\ |\ 0\leq i<\mathsf{len}(v)\}
. As
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})=\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
; so we prove the first part.

For showing that
\textup{{{init}}}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
, we observe that by the Assumption
1
, no other non-initial event in
\xi
writes on
x
in
\xi
. Hence,
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
contain less than
k
non-initial events. As
\textup{{{init}}}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)},[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
, and
\mathsf{OpSpec}
is maximally layered with layer bound
k
, we conclude that
\textup{{{init}}}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
.

For proving that
e_{0}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
but
(e_{0},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, we observe that by the Assumption
2
,
e_{0}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)},[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
. As
\mathsf{OpSpec}
is maximally layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
,
\textup{{{init}}}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
,
(\textup{{{init}}},e_{0})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
and
e_{0}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)},[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
; we conclude that
e_{0}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
.

For reaching a contradiction, we observe that
v
is non-arbitration-free. Hence, by the Assumption
3
,
(e_{0},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
. Once again, as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
(see
Definition

3.4
), we deduce that
e_{0}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
. However, as
e_{0}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
, we conclude that
\xi
is not valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
; which is contradicts the hypothesis. Thus, the layer bound of
\mathsf{OpSpec}
is bounded by the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
v
.
∎

Proposition B.18.

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}=(E,\mathsf{rspec},\mathsf{extract},\mathsf{wspec})
be an operation specification maximally layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
,
\mathsf{CMod}
be a consistency model in normal form w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
and
v
be a simple, conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, non-arbitration-free visibility formula. If the layer bound of
\mathsf{rspec}
is smaller or equal by the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
v
, then
v\not\in\mathsf{CMod}
.

Proof.

Let
v
be a simple, conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, non-arbitration-free visibility formula. We show that
v
is vacuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
; so
v\not\in\mathsf{CMod}
.

We reason by contradiction, assuming that
v
is non-vacuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
. In such case,
\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}\not\equiv_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}\mathsf{CMod}
but
\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}\preccurlyeq\mathsf{CMod}
. By
Proposition

B.6
, there exists an abstract execution on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, and object
x
, and events
w,r
s.t.
\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])\setminus\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}])
.

We observe that by Property
2
of
Definition

7.4
,
w\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
. Hence, as
w\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])\setminus\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}])
, we deduce that
v_{x}(w,r)
holds in
\xi
. As
v
is simple, there exist events
e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
s.t.
e_{0}=w
,
e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}=r
and
v_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\xi
.

First of all, as
\mathsf{rspec}
is maximally layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
and
e_{0}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(e_{0},[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
, every event in
\{e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}\}
that writes
x
is also in
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(e_{0},[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
. As
v
is conflict-maximal w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, at least
|E_{x}|
events write on
x
; where
E_{x}\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
s.t.
\mathsf{conflict}_{x}(E_{x})\in v
. Observe that for every event
e_{i}
s.t.
\varepsilon_{i}\in E_{x}
and
\mathsf{suff}_{x}(\mathsf{v}_{x},i)
is arbitration-free, as
\mathsf{CMod}
is closed under causal suffixes, there exists a visibility formula
v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{CMod}
s.t.
v^{\prime}_{x}(e_{i},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
. Thus,
|E_{x}|\geq\mathsf{af}(v)
, where
\mathsf{af}(v)
is the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
v
. Moreover, as
e_{0}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})(e_{0},[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])
, and
v
is not arbitration-free,
|E_{x}|>\mathsf{af}(v)
. However, as the layer bound of
\mathsf{rspec}
,
k
, is smaller or equal than the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
v
, the number of events read by
f_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
is at most
\mathsf{af}(v)
. Hence,
|E_{x}|\leq\mathsf{af}(v)
, which contradicts that
|E_{x}|>\mathsf{af}(v)
. We reach a contradiction; so the initial hypothesis, that
v
is non-vacuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
, is false. Thus,
v\not\in\mathsf{CMod}
.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of the Basic Arbitration-Free Consistency Theorem

Let in the folloeing
\mathsf{Spec}=(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
be a basic storage specification. We show that there exists an available
\mathsf{Spec}
-implementation iff
\mathsf{CMod}
is arbitration-free w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
.

C.1.
Arbitration-Freeness Implies Availability

As discussed in
Section

6
, the proof of such result is decomposed in three steps:

(1)

We show that arbitration-free consistency models w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
are weaker than
CC
(
Lemma

6.4
).

(2)

We deduce that available
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
-implementations are also available
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
-implementations as an immediate consequence of
Lemma

6.5
.

(3)

We prove that there exists available
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
-implementations (
Lemma

6.6
).

See
6.4

Proof.

For showing that
\mathsf{CMod}
is weaker than
CC
, let
h={({E,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
be a history and
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
be an abstract execution of
h
valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
. Let
n
be a consistency model in normal form that is
\mathsf{OpSpec}
-equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}
. By
Theorem

B.1
, such model always exists. As
\mathsf{CMod}
is arbitration-free, every visibility formula
v\in n
is arbitration-free. We conclude the result by showing that
n\preccurlyeq\textup{{CC}}
, i.e. showing that for every object
x
and every pair of distinct events
e,e^{\prime}\in E
, if
v_{x}(e,e^{\prime})
holds in
\xi
then
v^{\textup{{CC}}}_{x}(e,e^{\prime})
holds in
\xi
as well; where
v^{\textup{{CC}}}
is
Causal
, the visibility formula of
CC
(
Figure

4(b)
).

First, as
v_{x}(e,e^{\prime})
holds in
\xi
,
{e}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\ \mathsf{in}\ {\xi}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(e)\neq\emptyset
. Moreover, as
v
is simple, for every
i,1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\}
. By Property
2
of
Definition

3.4
, we deduce that
(e,e^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
. Altogether, we conclude that
v^{\textup{{CC}}}_{x}(e,e^{\prime})
holds in
\xi
.
∎

See
6.6

Proof.

We define an available implementation of
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}=(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

As discussed in
Section

5
, any implementation
I_{E}={({S_{\mathsf{i}},A_{\mathsf{i}},s_{0}^{\mathsf{i}},\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}})}
can be characterized by describing its set of states
S_{\mathsf{i}}
, its actions
A_{\mathsf{i}}
, its initial state
\sigma_{0}^{\mathsf{i}}
and its transition function
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}
.

First, we define
S_{\mathsf{i}}
as the set of possible values that each object may have; and the declare the initial state any possible state in
S_{\mathsf{i}}
. Next, we define
A_{\mathsf{i}}
via the synchronized actions
\mathsf{Events}\times(\mathsf{Objs}\times\mathsf{Events}\cup\{\emptyset\})
, as well as the local actions
\mathtt{send}
and
\mathtt{receive}
. We assume local actions are defined in a similar way to
\mathsf{Events}
, as tuples
a=(\mathsf{id},\mathsf{r},\mathsf{op},\mathsf{m})
, where
\mathsf{id}
is an action identifier,
\mathsf{r}
is a replica identifier,
\mathsf{op}
an operation identifier and
\mathsf{m}
is additional metadata of the action. As for events, we use
\mathtt{id}(a)
,
\mathtt{rep}(a)
,
\textup{{op}}(a)
and
\mathtt{md}(a)
for indicating the identifier, replica, operation and metadata of an action
a
.

For describing its transition function, we rely on the definition of
CC
. As we design
{({S_{\mathsf{i}},A_{\mathsf{i}},s_{0}^{\mathsf{i}},\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}})}
to be an available
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}
-implementation, we require that any induced abstract execution must be valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}
. However,
Definition

4.2
describes validity “a posteriori”, i.e. validity can only be checked once the event is executed; while transition functions describe validity “a priori”, i.e. describe a procedure to compute a write-read of a given, not yet added event. For solving this issue, we observe that under
CC
, that the context of an event
e
belonging to a synchronized action
a=(e,m)
only depends on (a) the transitive set of received actions before the last action in its replica and (b) the synchronized actions executed in its own replica. Ensuring transitive communication, i.e. ensuring that every send action on replica
r
transmits information about all synchronized actions executed or received on replica
r
before such
\mathtt{send}
action suffices to provide
CC
.

More in detail, for describing the transition function
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}(t,a)
, we require that (1)
a
is not present in
t
and (2) transitive communication is ensured. Also, we require a third condition depending on the type of
a
:

•

if
a
is a synchronized action, we require that (3a) if
a
represents a read operation,
a=(e,m)
, then
e
must read from the latest writing event w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
(which coincides with the trace order) received before
l_{r}^{t}
,

•

if
a
is a
\mathtt{send}
action, then (3b) it precedes a synchronized action, and

•

if
a
is a
\mathtt{receive}
action, then (3c) there exists a unique preceding
\mathtt{send}
action that matches it.

where
r=\mathtt{rep}(a)
and
l_{r}^{t}
to the last action in trace
t
whose replica is
r
.

On one hand, (1) ensures that
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}(t,e)
is well-defined, i.e. in every trace of
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}
, each action contains each action exactly once. On the other hand, (2) and (3a) ensure that
I_{E}
is a
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}
-storage implementation while (3b) and (3c) ensure that
I_{E}
is an available storage implementation.

Formally,
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}(t,a)\downarrow
if and only if
a\not\in t
and
\mathsf{sat}(t,a)
holds; and in such case
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}(t,a)=t\oplus a
. The predicate
\mathsf{sat}(t,a)
is described in
Equation

50
.

(50)

{\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathsf{sat}(t,a)&=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{llll}a=(e,M_{t}(e))&\text{if }\textup{{op}}(a)&\neq&\mathtt{send},\mathtt{receive}\\
\mathsf{sendIfData}(t,a)&\text{if }\textup{{op}}(a)&=&\mathtt{send}\\
\;\mathsf{sendAllData}(t,a)\\
\;\text{and }\mathsf{maxSend}(t,a)\\
\mathsf{minRcv}(t,a)&\text{if }\textup{{op}}(a)&=&\mathtt{receive}\\
\;\text{and }\mathsf{maxRcv}(t,a)\\
\end{array}}\right.\end{array}}

where
M_{t}(e)
is the mapping assigning to the objext
x=\mathsf{obj}({e})
the last event that writes on
x
received by
e
, formally defined using
Equations

51
and
52
; and the predicates
\mathsf{sendIfData}
,
\mathsf{sendAllData}
,
\mathsf{maxSend}
,
\mathsf{minRcv}
and
\mathsf{maxRcv}
are defined in
Sections

C.1
,
C.1
,
C.1
and
C.1
.

(51)

{\begin{array}[]{lll}M_{t}(e)&=&\left[x\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{e}^{t}}E^{x}_{t}(e)\}&\text{if }x=\mathsf{obj}({e})\\
\emptyset&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.\right]_{x\in\mathsf{Objs}}\\
E^{x}_{t}(e)&=&\left\{e^{\prime}\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Events}\cap t\ \land\ {e^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\ \mathsf{in}\ {\mathtt{exec}(t)}\ \land\\
(\mathtt{rep}(e^{\prime})=\mathtt{rep}(e)\lor\mathsf{rec}_{t}(e^{\prime},e))\end{array}\right.\right\}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{e}^{t}&=&{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{\restriction E^{x}_{t}(e)\times E^{x}_{t}(e)}\end{array}}

(52)

\mathsf{rec}_{t}(e^{\prime},e)=\exists r,s\in t\text{ s.t. }\bigwedge\begin{array}[]{l}\textup{{op}}(r)=\mathtt{receive},\mathtt{rep}(r)=\mathtt{rep}(e),\\
\textup{{op}}(s)=\mathtt{send},\mathtt{rep}(s)=\mathtt{rep}(e^{\prime}),\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(s)={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(r),e^{\prime}<_{t}s<_{t}r<e^{\prime}\end{array}

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathsf{sendIfData}(t,a)&\Coloneqq&\textup{{op}}(a^{\prime\prime})\neq\mathtt{send}\end{array}}

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lll}\;\text{where }a^{\prime\prime}&=&\max_{<_{t}}\left\{a^{\prime}\in t\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{rep}(a^{\prime})=\mathtt{rep}(a)\ \land\ \textup{{op}}(a^{\prime})\neq\mathtt{receive}\end{array}\right.\right\}\end{array}}

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathsf{sendAllData}(t,a)&\Coloneqq&\forall a^{\prime}\in t.\mathtt{rep}(a^{\prime})=\mathtt{rep}(a)\land\textup{{op}}(a^{\prime})\neq\mathtt{send}\\
&&\quad\qquad\implies\mathsf{RV}^{x}_{a^{\prime}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a)\\
\end{array}}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\;\text{where }\mathsf{RV}^{x}_{a^{\prime}}&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{e\}&\text{if }\textup{{op}}(a^{\prime})\neq\mathtt{send},\mathtt{receive}\ \land\\
&a^{\prime}=(e,\_)\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a^{\prime})&\text{if }\textup{{op}}(a^{\prime})=\mathtt{receive}\\
\emptyset&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{maxSend}(t,a)\Coloneqq&\nexists a^{\prime}\in t.\textup{{op}}(a^{\prime})=\mathtt{send}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a)={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a^{\prime})\\
\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{minRcv}(t,a)\Coloneqq&\exists a^{\prime}\in t.\textup{{op}}(a^{\prime})=\mathtt{send}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a)={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a^{\prime})\end{array}

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathsf{maxRcv}(t,a)\Coloneqq&\nexists a^{\prime}\in t.&\textup{{op}}(a^{\prime})=\mathtt{receive}\ \land\ \mathtt{rep}(a)=\mathtt{rep}(a^{\prime})\land{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a)={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a^{\prime})\\
\end{array}}

Note that as
I_{E}
contains
\mathtt{send}
and
\mathtt{receive}
actions, as well as events along with their write-read dependencies,
I_{E}
is a storage implementation.
For proving that
I_{E}
is the searched implementation, we introduce the following notation: for a trace
t
and an event
e\in t
,
\mathsf{prefix}(t,e)
to the trace s.t.
\Delta(\mathsf{prefix}(t,e),e)
is a prefix of
t
.

The rest of the proof, showing that
I_{E}
is an available
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}
-implementation, is a consequence of
Lemmas

C.1
,
C.2
and
C.3
.∎

The implementation

of at most length
k
,
\mathtt{exec}(t^{\prime})
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}
; and let us show that for any trace
t
of length
k+1
,
\mathtt{exec}(t)
is also valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}
. Let
h={({E,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
and
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
be respectively the induced history
\mathsf{history}(t)
and the induced abstract execution
\mathtt{exec}(t)
where
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
coincides with the trace order. We denote
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{sr}}
to the induced order between send-receive actions with the same
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
-
\mathsf{Set}
on
t
. Before proving that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}
, we show that
t
is feasible, i.e.
\xi
satisfies
Definition

3.4
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}};{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{*}
:
This is immediate by the definition of induced receive-before.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
:
By definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
, so we focus on proving that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
. Let
w,r
be events and
x
be an object s.t.
(w,r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
. In such case, there is a pair of actions
a_{r},a_{w}
s.t.
r\in a_{r}
,
w\in a_{w}
and
w\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a_{r})(x)
. Hence,
\{w\}=\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{e}^{t}}E^{x}_{t}(e)
. We deduce then that
\mathsf{rec}_{t}(w,r)
must hold; which implies that there exists a
\mathtt{send}
action
s
and a
\mathtt{receive}
action
v
s.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(s)={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(v)
and
w<_{t}s<_{t}v<_{t}r
. By
\mathsf{sendAllData}
predicate,
w\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(s)
. As
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(s)={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(v)
,
w\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(v)
. By the definition of induced abstract execution,
(w,r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
:
For proving that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
can be derived by
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{sr}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
, it suffices to prove that both
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{sr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. First, as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
is the partial order induced by the total order
<_{t}
on actions executed on the same replica,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
.

Next, for proving that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{sr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, let
s
be a
\mathtt{send}
action and let
v
be a
\mathtt{receive}
action s.t.
(s,v)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{sr}}
. Let us consider
p_{v}^{t}=\mathsf{prefix}(t,v)
be the prefix of
t
before
v
. On one hand, as
p_{v}^{t}
is a prefix of
t^{\prime}
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}(p_{v}^{t},v)\downarrow
.
In particular,
\mathsf{minRcv}(p_{v}^{t},v)
holds; so there is a
\mathtt{send}
action
s^{\prime}
in
p_{v}^{t}
s.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(s^{\prime})={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(v)
. We show that
s^{\prime}=s
. Otherwise, then w.l.o.g.
s<_{t}s^{\prime}
. Note that
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}(\mathsf{prefix}(t,s^{\prime}),s^{\prime})\downarrow
as
\mathsf{prefix}(t,s^{\prime})\oplus s^{\prime}
is a prefix of
t^{\prime}
. In such case,
\mathsf{maxSend}(\mathsf{prefix}(t,s^{\prime}),s^{\prime})
does not hold; which is impossible as
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}(\mathsf{prefix}(t,s^{\prime}),s^{\prime})\downarrow
. Therefore,
s=s^{\prime}
. As
s^{\prime}\in p_{v}^{t}
,
s
precedes
v
in
t
; so
(s,v)\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
.

After proving that
t
is feasible, we show that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\textup{{CC}}}
. By
Definition

4.2
, we need to show that for every event
r
and object
x
, if
\mathsf{rspec}(r)\uparrow
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)=\emptyset
, and otherwise,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)=\{\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}}\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\}
. Let
r
be a read event,
x
be the object it affects and
p=\mathsf{prefix}(t,r)
. We know by
Equation

51
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)=\{\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{r}^{p}}E^{x}_{p}(r)\}
. Observe then that by
Equation

51
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
’s definition,
E^{x}_{p}(r)=\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[t,\textup{{CC}}])
. Thus, we conclude that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)=\{\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}}\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\}
.
∎
