{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox to3.11pt{\vbox to0.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.80275pt\lower-0.35565pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.09 0 L 2.09 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@stroke@opacity{0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-opacity} \lxSVG@fill@opacity{0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill-opacity} {}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@stroke@opacity{0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-opacity} \lxSVG@fill@opacity{0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill-opacity} {}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -0.83 0 L 2.92 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@stroke@opacity{0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-opacity} \lxSVG@fill@opacity{0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill-opacity} {}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -0.83 0 L 2.92 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}}I_{E}},t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{{P_{E}}},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}_{I_{E}}
be traces s.t.
t=(t_{1},t_{2})
. As
{P_{E}}
is finite, the length of
t_{1}
,
\mathsf{len}(t_{1})
, is finite. We show that
k_{t}\Coloneqq 3\cdot\mathsf{len}(t_{1})
is the constant we search.

Three cases arise, depending on the type of action we consider. First, by
\mathsf{maxRcv}
predicate, the number of
\mathtt{receive}
actions coincides with the number of
\mathtt{receive}
actions with distinct metadata; which by
\mathsf{minRcv}
, is bounded by the number of
\mathtt{send}
actions in the trace. Then, by
\mathsf{sendIfData}
, the number of
\mathtt{send}
actions is bounded by the number of synchronized actions. Finally, by the parallel composition definition, the number of synchronized actions in
t
and
t_{1}
coincide; so such number is bounded by
\mathsf{len}(t_{1})
. Altogether, we deduce that
\mathsf{len}(t)\leq 3\cdot\mathsf{len}(t_{1})=k_{t}
. ∎

C.2.
Availability Implies Arbitration-Freeness

As explained in
Section

6
, we prove the contrapositive: if
\mathsf{CMod}
is not arbitration-free, then no available
\mathsf{Spec}
-implementation exists. Indeed, if
\mathsf{CMod}
is not arbitration-free, every normal form
\mathsf{CMod}^{\prime}
of
\mathsf{CMod}
contains a simple visibility formula involving
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
(see
Definition

6.2
). By
Lemma

6.7
, such a formula precludes the existence of an available
(\mathsf{CMod}^{\prime},\mathsf{OpSpec})
-implementation. Consequently, there is no available
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
-implementation, since any such implementation would also be an available
(\mathsf{CMod}^{\prime},\mathsf{OpSpec})
-implementation – this is an easy observation as
\mathsf{CMod}
is equivalent to
\mathsf{CMod}^{\prime}
(see
Theorem

B.1
).

Proof.

We assume by contradiction that there is an available implementation
I_{E}
of
\mathsf{Spec}

but
\mathsf{CMod}
contains a visibility formula
v
s.t. for some
i,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
.
We use the latter fact to construct a specific program, which by the assumption, admits a trace (in the composition with this implementation) that contains no
\mathtt{receive}
action. We show that any abstract execution induced by this trace, which is admissible by any available implementation of
\mathsf{Spec}
, is not valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
. This contradicts the hypothesis.

The program
P
we construct generalizes the litmus program presented in
Figure

1
.
P
uses two replicas
r_{0},r_{1}
, two distinguished objects
x_{0},x_{1}
and a collection of events
e_{i}^{x_{l}},0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v),l\in\{0,1\}
. The events are used to “encode” two instances
v_{x_{0}}
and
v_{x_{1}}
of the visibility formula.

Let
d_{v}
be the largest index
i
s.t.
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
(last occurrence of
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
). Then,
v
is formed of two parts: the path of dependencies from
\varepsilon_{0}
to
\varepsilon_{d_{v}}
which is not arbitration-free, and the suffix from
\varepsilon_{d_{v}}
up to
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
, the arbitration-free part. Thus,
\mathsf{v}
is of the form:

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{x}(\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\Coloneqq\exists\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n-1}.

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{i=1}^{\mathsf{len}(v)}(\varepsilon_{i-1},\varepsilon_{i})\in\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{v}}_{i}\ \land{\varepsilon_{0}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(\varepsilon_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\neq\emptyset

where
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{v}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}\}
, for all
i<d_{v}
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{d_{v}}^{v}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{v}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\}
for all
i>d_{v}
.

In the construction,
we require that replica
r_{l}
executes events
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
if
i<d_{v}
and events
e_{i}^{x_{1-l}}
otherwise – the replica
r_{l}
executes the non arbitration-free part of
v
for object
x_{l}
and the arbitration-free suffix of
v
for
x_{1-l}
. All objects in replica
r_{l}
access (read and/or write)
x_{l}
except
e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}}
, which access with
x_{1-l}
. We denote by
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
to the unique object that event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
reads and/or writes.

More in detail, we construct a set of events,
E^{i}
, histories,
h^{i}={({E^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}})}
, and executions,
\xi^{i}={({h^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}})}
,
0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
inductively, starting from an initial event
init
, and incorporating at each time a pair of new events,
e_{i}^{x_{0}}
and
e_{i}^{x_{1}}
. For simplifying notation, we use the convention
\textup{{{init}}}=e_{-1}^{x_{0}}=e_{-1}^{x_{1}}
.

For the inductive step, we assume that the abstract execution
\xi^{i-1}={({h^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{-1}})}
associated to the history
h^{i-1}={({E^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i-1}})}
contains events
e_{-1}^{x_{0}}\ldots e_{i-1}^{x_{0}},e_{i-1}^{x_{1}}
and is well-defined (satisfies
Definition

3.4
) and we construct the history
h^{i}
and the abstract execution
\xi^{i}
.

We distinguish between cases depending on the value
i
:

•

i=0
: In this case, we consider
e_{0}
be an event s.t.
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{0}^{x_{l}})(\mathtt{wval}(\textup{{{init}}})(\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}))\downarrow
.

•

0<i<\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i+1}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
: In this case, it is easy to see that by
Proposition

B.11
,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows atomic read-write events. We consider
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
be an event s.t.
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})\downarrow
and
\mathsf{wspec}(w_{i}^{x_{l}})(\mathtt{value}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i-1}}(w_{i}^{x_{l}},\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}))\downarrow
.

•

0<i<\mathsf{len}(v)
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i+1}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
: In this case, if
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows unconditional writes, then we select
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
as an unconditional write event on object
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
. Otherwise, we select event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
s.t.
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})\downarrow
and
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(\mathtt{wval}(w_{i}^{x_{l}})(\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}))\downarrow
.

•

0<i<\mathsf{len}(v)
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i+1}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
: In this case, we select
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
to not write
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
unless it is necessary. If
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows read events that are not write events, or if allows conditional atomic read-write events, we select
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
as an event such that
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})\downarrow
but
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(\mathtt{wval}(w_{i}^{x_{l}})(\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}))\uparrow
. Otherwise, we select event
e_{i}
such that
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})\downarrow
and
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(\mathtt{wval}(w_{i}^{x_{l}})(\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}))\downarrow
.

•

i=\mathsf{len}(v)
: In this case, we consider
e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}}
to be an event that reads object
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
, i.e.
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}})\downarrow
.

where
l\in\{0,1\}
and
w_{i}^{x_{l}}=\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i-1}}\{e\in E^{i-1}\ |\ \mathsf{wspec}(e)(\mathsf{obj}({e_{i}^{x_{l}}}))\downarrow\land\ (e,e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}\}
. We note that as
init
writes on every object,
w_{i}^{x_{l}}
is well-defined.

First of all, observe that event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is well-defined thanks to
Lemma

C.4
and the assumptions on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
(
Section

4.3
). We denote
E^{i}=E^{i-1}\cup\{e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}}\}
. We observe that w.l.o.g., we can assume that the
\mathtt{id}(e_{i}^{x_{0}})
is bigger than every identifier of an event in
E^{i-1}
and that
\mathtt{id}(e_{i}^{x_{0}})<\mathtt{id}(e_{i}^{x_{1}})
.

We conclude the description of
h^{i}
and
\xi^{i}
by specifying the relations
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
. We require that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
(resp.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
) contains
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i-1}
(resp.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i-1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i-1}
). Also, we require additional constrains on them due to event
e_{i}
:

•

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
: We require that
(e,e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
iff
\mathtt{rep}(e)=\mathtt{rep}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})
; as well as
(\textup{{{init}}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}
: If
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is not a read event, we require that
{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x_{i}}^{i}}^{-1}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})\neq\emptyset
. Otherwise, we require that
(\{w_{i}^{x_{l}}\},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{x_{i}}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
: We require that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
: We impose that for every event
e\in E^{i}
,
(e,e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
. Also, we impose that
(e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
.

Then, we define
\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}=E^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
as the set our program employs. The set
\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
induces the set of traces
\mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{p}}
.

We define the program
P={({S_{\mathsf{p}},A_{\mathsf{p}},s_{0}^{\mathsf{p}},\Delta_{\mathsf{p}}})}
, where
\textup{{{init}}}_{\mathsf{p}}=\textup{{{init}}}
and
\Delta_{\mathsf{p}}
is the transition function defined as follows: for every trace
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{p}}
and event
e\in\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{p}}(t,e)\downarrow
if and only if
e\not\in t
and every event in
\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
whose replica coincide with
e
and has smaller identifier than
e
is included in
t
.

Given such a program
P
, the proof proceeds as follows:

(1)

There exists a finite trace
t
of
P\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox to4.27pt{\vbox to0.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.06108pt\lower-0.35565pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {{

\lxSVG@closescope

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}}I_{E}
that contains no receive action (
Lemma

C.5
): Since
I_{E}
is available, it can always delay receiving messages, and execute other actions instead. Then, as
P
is a finite program, such an execution must be finite.

(2)

The trace
t
induces a history
h_{\mathsf{v}}={({E,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
and an abstract execution
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
(
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
is arbitrary as long as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
). As
I_{E}
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
,
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
. Next, we prove that since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
, events in
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
read the latest value w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
written on their associated object in
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
(
Lemma

C.6
). In particular, we deduce that all traces of
P
without
\mathtt{receive}
events induce the same history and therefore, the induced history does not change when the induced arbitration order changes.

(3)

Since
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
is a total order, either
(e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{0}},e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{1}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
or
(e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{1}},e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{0}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. W.l.o.g., assume that
(e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{0}},e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{1}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. By
Lemma

C.7
, we deduce that
e_{0}^{x_{0}}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x_{0}}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}},[\xi_{\mathsf{v}},\mathsf{CMod}])
. The proof is explained in
Figure

5
: if
(e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{0}},e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{1}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, then all events
e_{i}^{x_{0}}
form a path in such way that
\mathsf{v}_{x_{0}}(e_{0}^{x_{0}},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}})
holds in
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
.

(4)

Since
e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}}
is the only event at
r_{1}
that reads or writes
x_{0}
and events in
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
read the latests values w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
in
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
, we deduce that
e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}}
reads
x_{0}
from
init
. However, as
e_{0}^{x_{0}}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x_{0}}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}},[\xi_{\mathsf{v}},\mathsf{CMod}])
and
init
precedes
e_{0}^{x_{0}}
in arbitration order, we deduce that
e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}}
does not read the latest value w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, i.e.
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}})\downarrow
but
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x_{0}}^{-1}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}})\neq\{\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}}\mathsf{ctxt}_{x_{0}}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}},[\xi_{\mathsf{v}},\mathsf{CMod}])\}
. Therefore,
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
is not valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
(see
Definition

4.2
). This contradicts the hypothesis that
I_{E}
is an implementation of
\mathsf{Spec}
. ∎

Lemma C.4.

Let
\mathsf{Spec}=(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
be a storage specification s.t.
\mathsf{CMod}
is in normal form w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
. For every visibility formula
v\in\mathsf{CMod}
, there exists an abstract execution valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
,
\xi
, an object
x
and events
e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
s.t.
\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\downarrow
and
v_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\xi
.

Proof.

Let
v\in\mathsf{CMod}
be a visibility formula. As
\mathsf{CMod}
is normal form w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
,
v
is non-vacuous; so
\mathsf{CMod}\not\equiv\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}
. Hence, there exists an abstract execution valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
,
\xi
, an object
x
and a read event
r
s.t.
\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}])\neq\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}])
. As
\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\}\preccurlyeq\mathsf{CMod}
, we conclude that there exists events
e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
s.t.
r=e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
and
v_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\xi
.
∎

s.t.

(1)

t
does not contain any action
a
s.t.
\textup{{op}}(a)=\mathtt{receive}
.

(2)

for every event
e\in\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
there exists exactly one action
a\in t
s.t.
\mathtt{ev}(a)=e
and,

(3)

for every two actions
a,a^{\prime}\in t
in the same replica, if
\mathtt{ev}(a)\downarrow
,
\mathtt{ev}(a^{\prime})\downarrow
and
\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{ev}(a))<\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{ev}(a^{\prime}))
, then
a<_{t}a^{\prime}

Proof.

Let
I_{E}
be an available storage implementation. We construct a sequence of traces
\{t^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
s.t. for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
(1)
t^{i}
does not contain any
\mathtt{receive}
action, (2a) for every event
e\in\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
s.t.
\mathtt{id}(e)\leq\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{last}_{\mathtt{rep}(e)}(\pi_{1}(t^{i})))
there is exactly one action
a\in t^{i}
s.t.
\mathtt{ev}(a)=e
, (2b) for every event
e\in\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
s.t.
\mathtt{id}(e)>\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{last}_{\mathtt{rep}(e)}(\pi_{1}(t^{i})))
there is no action
a\in t^{i}
s.t.
\mathtt{ev}(a)=e
, and (3) for every two actions
a,a^{\prime}\in t
, if
\mathtt{ev}(a)\downarrow
,
\mathtt{ev}(a^{\prime})\downarrow
and
\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{ev}(a))<\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{ev}(a^{\prime}))
, then
a<_{t^{i}}a^{\prime}
.

Let
t^{0}=\textup{{{init}}}_{P\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox to3.11pt{\vbox to0.71pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.80275pt\lower-0.35565pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}

By induction hypothesis on
t^{n}
,
t^{n}
satisfies properties (1), (2a), (2b) and (3). We show that
t^{n+1}
also satisfies (1), (2a), (2b) and (3). Without loss of generality, we assume that
t^{n+1}\neq t^{n}
as otherwise the result immediately holds. First, as
t^{n}
satisfies (1) and
a_{n}
is not a
\mathtt{receive}
action,
t^{n+1}

.

Finally, for proving that
t^{n+1}
satisfies (3), let
a,a^{\prime}\in t^{n}
be distinct actions s.t.
\mathtt{ev}(a)\downarrow
,
\mathtt{ev}(a^{\prime})\downarrow
and
\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{ev}(a))<\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{ev}(a^{\prime}))
. If
a,a^{\prime}\neq a_{n}
, as
t^{n}
satisfies (3),
a<_{t^{n}}a^{\prime}
and therefore,
a<_{t^{n+1}}a^{\prime}
. Otherwise, note that as
t^{n}
satisfies (2b), for every event
e\in\pi_{1}(t^{n})
,
\mathtt{id}(e)\leq\mathtt{id}(\mathtt{ev}(a_{n}))
. Moreover, as no two events in
\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
have identical identifier, traces do not contain the same event twice and
a\neq a^{\prime}
, we deduce that
a^{\prime}=a_{n}
. As
a_{n}=\mathtt{last}_{r_{n}}(t^{n+1})
, we conclude that
a<_{t^{n+1}}a^{\prime}
.

t^{\infty}
is a trace in

is finite. Thus,
t^{\infty}
must be finite; which contradicts its construction. Hence, such
k
exists.

We show that the trace
t^{k}
is the searched trace. Clearly, as
t^{k}
satisfies (1) and (3), it suffices to prove that it also satisfies (2). On one hand, as
t^{k}=t^{k+1}
, for every event
e\in P
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{p}}(\pi_{1}(t^{k}),e)\uparrow
. Hence, for every replica
r_{l},l\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathtt{last}_{r}(\pi_{1}(t^{k}))=e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{1-l}}
. By construction of
\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
, every event
e\in\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
with replica
r_{l}
has smaller identifier than
e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{1-l}}
. Therefore, as
t^{k}
satisfies (2a), there is exactly one action
a^{\prime}\in t^{k}
s.t.
\mathtt{ev}(e^{\prime})=e
; so
t^{k}
satisfies (2).

∎

Lemma C.6.

For every pair of indices
i,-1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
l\in\{0,1\}
,

•

If
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a read event, then
(\{w_{i}^{x_{l}}\},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}}
.

•

If
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a write event s.t.
\mathtt{wval}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}})\downarrow
, then
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(\mathtt{wval}(w_{i}^{x_{l}})(\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}))\downarrow
.

Proof.

We prove the result by induction on
i
; where the base case,
i=-1
, trivially holds. For showing the inductive case, let us assume that the result holds for every event
e_{i^{\prime}}^{x_{l^{\prime}}},-1\leq i^{\prime}<i,l^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
, and let us show it for events
e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}}
. We divide the proof in two blocks, whether
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a read event, and
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a write event.

For the first part, we note that by construction of
\xi_{v}
using
Lemma

C.5
we know that
\xi_{v}
does not contain any
\mathtt{receive}
event,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
. Hence, as
\xi_{v}
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
. Thus,
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
reads
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
from an event that precedes it in session order. In particular, by
Definition

4.2
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}}^{-1}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})=\{\max_{{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}}\mathsf{ctxt}_{\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}}(e_{i}^{x_{l}},[\xi_{v},\mathsf{CMod}])\}
; so
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}}^{-1}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})=\{w_{i}^{x_{l}}\}
.

For the second part, we can assume w.l.o.g. that
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a conditional write, as otherwise the result immediately holds. By the choice of
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
, in this case, we conclude that
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(\mathtt{wval}(w_{i}^{x_{l}})(\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}))\downarrow
.

∎

Lemma C.7.

For every
l\in\{0,1\}
, if
(e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{l}},e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{1-l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, then
e_{0}^{x_{l}}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x_{l}}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}},[\xi_{v},\mathsf{CMod}])
.

Proof.

For proving that
e_{0}^{x_{l}}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x_{l}}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}},[\xi_{v},\mathsf{CMod}])
, we show that
v_{x_{l}}(e_{0}^{x_{l}},e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}})
holds in
\xi_{v}
. Observe that by the choice of events and
Lemma

C.6

{e_{0}^{x_{l}}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x_{l}}\ \mathsf{in}\ {\xi_{v}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x_{l}}^{-1}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}})\neq\emptyset
holds in
\xi_{v}
. Therefore, to conclude the result, we prove that for every
i,1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{v}
.

For proving it, we observe that
\mathsf{CMod}
is in simple form. Thus, for every
i,1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
is either
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
; which simplify our analysis. First, if
i=d_{v}
, by definition of
d_{v}
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. By hypothesis,
(e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{l}},e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{1-l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. In such case, as
\mathtt{id}(e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{1-l}})<\mathtt{id}(e_{d_{v}}^{x_{l}})
and
\mathtt{rep}(e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{1-l}})=\mathtt{rep}(e_{d_{v}}^{x_{l}})
,
(e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{1-l}},e_{d_{v}}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
. Therefore, as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
is a transitive relation, we deduce that
(e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{l}},e_{d_{v}}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
.

Next, if
i\neq d_{v}
, we notice that
(e_{i-1}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{0}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. Hence, if
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
is either
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, the result immediately holds. Otherwise, if
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{v}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, we show that
e_{i}^{x_{0}}
is a read event and
e_{i-1}^{x_{0}}=w_{i}^{x_{0}}
; which let us conclude that
(e_{i-1}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{0}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
thanks to
Lemma

C.6
.

First, we show that if
i\neq\mathsf{len}(v)
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, then
w_{i}^{x_{l}}=e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
. Thanks to the choice of
P
, if
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, then
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a write event s.t.
\mathtt{wval}(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}})(\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}})\downarrow
. By
Lemma

C.6
, we deduce that
{e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {\tilde{x}_{i-1}^{x_{l}}}
in
\xi_{v}
. As
i\neq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\tilde{x}_{i-1}^{x_{l}}=\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
. Also, as
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
,
\mathtt{rep}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})=\mathtt{rep}(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}})
. Altogether, we deduce that
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
is an event writing
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
that is the immediate predecessor of
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
. Hence,
w_{i}^{x_{l}}=e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
.

Finally, we show that
\mathsf{Rel}_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{v}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and conclude the result. We prove the contrapositive, that if
\mathsf{Rel}_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
,
v
is vacuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
. If
\mathsf{Rel}_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, for every abstract execution
\xi^{\prime}
valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
, object
x
and a collection of events
f_{0},\ldots f_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
, if
v_{x}(f_{0},\ldots,f_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in
\xi^{\prime}
, then
(f_{\mathsf{len}(v)-1},f_{\mathsf{len}(v)})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
. Thus,
\xi^{\prime}
is valid w.r.t.
(\mathsf{CMod}\setminus\{v\},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Hence,
v
is vacuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of the Arbitration-Free Consistency Theorem

Lemmas

D.1
and
8.2
prove the AFC theorem.

D.1.
Arbitration-Freeness Implies Availability

The proof of (1)
\implies
(2), essentially coincides with that of
Lemma

6.6
: we present an available
\mathsf{Spec}
-implementation that guarantees
CC
. As in
Lemma

6.6
,
\mathsf{CMod}
is arbitration-free, so by
Lemma

6.4
, this implies that
\mathsf{CMod}
is weaker than
CC
. Thanks to
Lemma

7.11
, any implementation of
CC
also ensures
\mathsf{CMod}
.

Lemma D.1 ((1)
\implies
(2)).

Let
\mathsf{OpSpec}
be a basic operation specification. There exists an available
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
-implementation.

Proof.

The main difference in the construction w.r.t. the implementation shown in
Lemma

6.6
corresponds to the transition function,
\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}
. More specifically, the main and only change arise in
Equation

51
, which is substituted by
Equation

64
.

(64)

{\begin{array}[]{lll}M_{t}(e)&=&[x\mapsto\mathsf{rspec}(e)(x,E_{t}^{x}(e))]_{x\in\mathsf{Objs}}\\
E_{t}^{x}(e)&=&\left\{e^{\prime}\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Events}\cap t\ \land\ {e^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {x}\ \mathsf{in}\ {\mathtt{exec}(t)}\ \land\\
(\mathtt{rep}(e^{\prime})=\mathtt{rep}(e)\lor\mathsf{rec}_{t}(e^{\prime},e))\end{array}\right.\right\}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{e}^{t}&=&{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{\restriction E_{t}^{x}(e)\times E_{t}^{x}(e)}\end{array}}

Is immediate to show that
I_{E}
is a storage implementation. Showing that
I_{E}
is an available
\mathsf{Spec}
-implementation is done as in
Lemma

6.6
. Observe that
Lemmas

C.2
and
C.3
apply to this implementation; so
{({S_{\mathsf{i}},A_{\mathsf{i}},s_{0}^{\mathsf{i}},\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}})}
is an available implementation. In
Lemma

D.2
we show that indeed
I_{E}
is an implementation of
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{Spec})
, concluding the result.

∎

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@stroke@opacity{0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-opacity} \lxSVG@fill@opacity{0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill-opacity} {}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -0.83 0 L 2.92 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}}I_{E}}
of at most length
k
,
\mathtt{exec}(t^{\prime})
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\prime}
; and let us show that for any trace
t
of length
k+1
,
\mathtt{exec}(t)
is also valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\prime}
.

Let
h={({E,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
and
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
be respectively the history
\mathsf{history}(t)
and the abstract execution
\mathtt{exec}(t)
. We denote
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{sr}}
to the induced order between send-receive actions with the same metadata on
t
. For proving that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\prime}
, we first prove that
\xi
is indeed an abstract execution, i.e.,
\xi
satisfies
Definition

3.4
. In particular, by the construction of
{({S_{\mathsf{i}},A_{\mathsf{i}},s_{0}^{\mathsf{i}},\Delta_{\mathsf{i}}})}
(compared with that of
Lemma

C.1
), it suffices showing that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
.

By definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
, so we focus on proving that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
. Let
w,r
be events and
x
be an object s.t.
(w,r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}
. In such case, there is a pair of actions
a_{r},a_{w}
s.t.
r\in a_{r}
,
w\in a_{w}
and
w\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(a_{r})(x)
. Hence,
w\in\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,E_{t}^{x}(r))
. We deduce then that
\mathsf{rec}_{t}(w,r)
must hold; which implies that there exists a
\mathtt{send}
action
s
and a
\mathtt{receive}
action
v
s.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(s)={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(v)
and
w<_{t}s<_{t}v<_{t}r
. By
\mathsf{sendAllData}
predicate,
w\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(s)
; so by
\mathsf{minRcv}
,
w\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}\mathchar 45\relax\mathtt{Set}(v)
. By the induced abstract execution definition,
(w,r)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
.

Finally, we show that
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}^{\prime}
. By
Definition

7.8
, it suffices to show that for every event
r
and object
x
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)=\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}]))
. Let
r
be a read event,
x
be an object and
p=\mathsf{prefix}(t,r)
. We know by
Equation

64
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)=\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,E_{p}^{x}(r))
. Observe that by
Equation

64
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
’s definition,
E_{p}^{x}(r)
coincides with
\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[t,\textup{{CC}}])
. Thus, so we conclude that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{x}^{-1}(r)=\mathsf{rspec}(r)(x,\mathsf{ctxt}_{x}(r,[t,\textup{{CC}}]))
.
∎

D.2.
Availability Implies Arbitration-Freeness

The proof of this result mimics that of
Lemma

6.7
. We prove the contrapositive: if
\mathsf{CMod}
is not arbitration-free, then no available
\mathsf{Spec}
-implementation exists. Indeed, if
\mathsf{CMod}
is not arbitration-free, every normal form
\mathsf{CMod}^{\prime}
of
\mathsf{CMod}
contains a simple visibility formula involving
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, and such formula precludes the existence of an available
(\mathsf{CMod},\mathsf{OpSpec})
-implementation (see
Lemma

8.2
).

See
8.2

Proof.

We assume by contradiction that there is an available implementation
I_{E}
of
\mathsf{Spec}

but
\mathsf{CMod}
contains a visibility formula
v
non-vacuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
s.t. for some
i,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. We use the latter fact to construct a specific program, which by the assumption, admits a trace (in the composition with this implementation) that contains no
\mathtt{receive}
action. We show that any abstract execution induced by this trace, which is admissible by any available implementation of
\mathsf{Spec}
, is not valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
. This contradicts the hypothesis.

The program
P
we construct generalizes the litmus program presented in
Figure

1
.
P
uses two replicas
r_{0},r_{1}
, two distinguished objects
x_{0},x_{1}
and a collection of events
e_{i}^{x_{l}},0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v),l\in\{0,1\}
. The events are used to “encode” two instances of
v_{x_{0}}
and
v_{x_{1}}
.

Let
d_{v}
be the largest index
i
s.t.
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
(last occurrence of
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
). Then,
v
is formed of two parts: the path of dependencies from
\varepsilon_{0}
to
\varepsilon_{d_{v}}
which is not arbitration-free, and the suffix from
\varepsilon_{d_{v}}
up to
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{len}(v)}
, the arbitration-free part.

For ensuring that
\mathsf{v}_{x}(e_{0}^{x_{l}},\ldots e_{n}^{x_{l}})
holds in an induced abstract execution of a trace without
\mathtt{receive}
actions, we require that if
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, then
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
is a write event and
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a read event. For ensuring that
\mathsf{wrCons}^{\mathsf{v}}_{x}(e_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
holds in such abstract execution, we consider a distinct object
y_{E}
, also distinct from
x_{0},x_{1}
. These objects represents each different conflict in
v
in an explicit manner. Intuitively, we require that events
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
write on object
y_{E}
(resp.
x_{l}
) iff
\varepsilon_{i}\in E
.

More formally, we denote by
E_{x}\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon_{0},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
to the set s.t.
\mathsf{conflict}_{x}(E_{x})\in v
. Also, for every
i,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v),l\in\{0,1\}
, we denote by
X_{i}^{x_{l}}
to the set containing objects
y_{E}
(resp.
\hat{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
) iff
E\in\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}
(resp.
E_{x}\in\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}
); where
\hat{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}=x_{l}
if
i<d_{v}
and
x_{1-l}
otherwise. We denote by
X
to the union of sets
X_{i}^{x_{l}},0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v),l\in\{0,1\}
.

In the construction,
we require that replica
r_{l}
executes events
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
if
i<d_{v}
and events
e_{i}^{x_{1-l}}
otherwise – the replica
r_{l}
executes the non arbitration-free part of
v
for object
x_{l}
and the arbitration-free suffix of
v
for
x_{1-l}
. We denote by
r_{i}^{x_{l}}
to such replica.

More in detail, we construct a set of events,
E^{i}
, histories,
h^{i}={({E^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}})}
, and executions,
\xi^{i}={({h^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}})}
,
0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
inductively, starting from an initial event
init
, and incorporating at each time a pair of new events,
e_{i}^{x_{0}}
and
e_{i}^{x_{1}}
. We use the notation
h^{-1}
and
\xi^{-1}
to describe the history and abstract execution containing only
init
respectively. For simplifying notation, we use the convention
\textup{{{init}}}=e_{-1}^{x_{0}}=e_{-1}^{x_{1}}
.

For the inductive step, we assume that the abstract execution
\xi^{i-1}={({h^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{-1}})}
associated to the history
h^{i-1}={({E^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i-1}})}
contains events
e_{-1}^{x_{0}}\ldots e_{i-1}^{x_{0}},e_{i-1}^{x_{1}}
and is well-defined (satisfies
Definition

3.4
) and we construct the history
h^{i}
and the abstract execution
\xi^{i}
.

The construction of
\xi^{i}
follows the structure of that constructed in
Lemma

6.7
’s proof, but with the technical details of that used in
Theorem

B.9
’s proof.

For the inductive step, we assume that the abstract execution
\xi^{i-1}={({h^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{-1}})}
associated to the history
h^{i-1}={({E^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i-1}})}
contains events
e_{-1}^{x_{0}}\ldots e_{i-1}^{x_{0}}e_{i-1}^{x_{1}}
and is well-defined (satisfies
Definition

3.4
) and we construct the history
h^{i}
and the abstract execution
\xi^{i}
.

In the following, let
l\in\{0,1\}
.

Like in
Theorem

B.9
, we define a pair of special objects,
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
and
o_{i}^{x_{l}}
. The purpose of object
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
is control the number of events in
\xi
that write object
x_{l}
.
Equation

65
describes
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
; where
\mathsf{choice}
is a function that deterministically chooses an element from a non-empty set. The object
o_{i}^{x_{l}}
is an object different from objects
x,y_{E},E\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon_{0},\ldots\varepsilon_{\mathsf{len}(v)})
and
o_{j}^{x_{l^{\prime}}},-1\leq j<i,l^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
that we use for ensuring that if
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, then
(e_{i-1},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
. W.l.o.g., we can assume that
o_{i}^{x_{0}}\neq o_{i}^{x_{1}}
.

(65)

\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tilde{x}_{i-1}^{x_{l}}&\text{if }X_{i}^{x_{l}}=\emptyset\\
\hat{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}&\text{if }X_{i}^{x_{l}}\neq\emptyset\text{ and }\hat{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}\in X_{i}^{x_{l}}\\
\mathsf{choice}\left(X_{i}\right)&\text{if }X_{i}^{x_{l}}\neq\emptyset\text{ and }\hat{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}\not\in X_{i}^{x_{l}}\end{array}\right.

We select a domain
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
, a set of objects
W_{i}^{x_{l}},W_{i}^{x_{l}}\subseteq D_{i}^{x_{l}}
that event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
must write, and a set of objects
C_{i}^{x_{l}}\subseteq D_{i}^{x_{l}}
whose value needs to be corrected for events
e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}}
in
\xi_{i+1}
– in the sense of
Definition

7.13
. We distinguishing between several cases:

•

i=0
or
0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
: In this case, we select
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
to be a write event. If
\mathsf{OpSpec}
only allows single-object atomic read-write events, we define
D_{i}^{x_{l}}=X_{i}^{x_{l}}
; while if not, we consider a domain containing
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}},o_{i}^{x_{l}}
, every object in
X_{i}^{x_{l}}
but no object from
X\setminus X_{i}^{x_{l}}
nor objects
o_{j}^{x_{l^{\prime}}},0\leq j<\mathsf{len}(v),l^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}j\neq i-1,i
. Observe that by
Proposition

B.10
, such domain always exist on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
.

If there is an unconditional write event whose domain is
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
, we define
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=D_{i}^{x_{l}}
. Otherwise, we define
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=X_{i}^{x_{l}}\cup\{o_{i}^{x_{l}}\}
.

•

0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
and
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
: In this case, by
Proposition

B.11
,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows atomic read-write events. If
\mathsf{OpSpec}
only allows single-object atomic read-write events, we define
D_{i}^{x_{l}}=X_{i}^{x_{l}}
; while if not, we consider a domain containing
o_{i-1},o_{i}
, every object in
X_{i}^{x_{l}}
but no object from
X\setminus X_{i}^{x_{l}}
nor objects
o_{j},0\leq j<\mathsf{len}(v),j\neq i-1,i
. Observe that by
Proposition

B.10
, such domain always exist on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
.

Similarly to the previous case, if there is an unconditional atomic read-write event whose domain is
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
, we define
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=D_{i}^{x_{l}}
. Otherwise, we define
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=X_{i}^{x_{l}}\cup\{o_{i}^{x_{l}}\}
.

•

0<i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
and
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}=\emptyset
: In this case, by
Proposition

B.11
,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows events that do not unconditionally write. If
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows read events that are not write events, we select
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
to be the domain of any such event and
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=\emptyset
. Otherwise,
\mathsf{OpSpec}
must allow conditional write events; so we select
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
to be the domain of any such event,
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=\emptyset
. Observe that in this case, thanks to the assumptions on
\mathsf{OpSpec}
(see
Section

7.4
), we can assume without loss of generality that whenever
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i-1}
,
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}
as well; while otherwise, that
\tilde{x}_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}
.

Finally we describe the event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
thanks to the sets
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
and
W_{i}^{x_{l}}
. If
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=D_{i}^{x_{l}}
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, we select an unconditional atomic read-write event whose domain is
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
. If
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=D_{i}^{x_{l}}
and
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
, we select an unconditional write event whose domain is
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
. If
W_{i}^{x_{l}}=\emptyset
and
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows read events that are not write events, we select a read event whose domain is
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
. Finally, if that is not the case, we select a conditional write event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
s.t.
\mathsf{obj}({e_{i}^{x_{l}}})=D_{i}^{x_{l}}
and s.t. an execution-corrector exists for
(e_{i}^{x_{l}},W_{i}^{x_{l}},\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}},\xi^{i-1}\oplus e_{i}^{x_{0}}\oplus e_{i}^{x_{1}})
. Such event always exists by the assumptions on operation specifications (
Section

7.4
). W.l.o.g. we can assume that
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
happens on replica
r_{i}^{x_{l}}
.

For concluding the description of
h^{i}={({E_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}})}
and
\xi^{i}={({h^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}})}
, we use an auxiliary history and abstract execution,
h^{i}_{-1}={({E^{i}_{-1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{-1}})}
and
\xi^{i}_{-1}={({h^{i}_{-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}_{-1},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}_{-1}})}
respectively. For specifying
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{-1}
, we define the context mapping
c^{i}:\mathsf{Objs}\to\mathsf{Contexts}
in the same fashion as in
Theorem

B.9
:

(66)

c_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)=(F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y),{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1}_{\restriction F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)\times F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i-1}_{\restriction F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)\times F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)})

where
F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)
is the mapping associating each object
y
with the set of events described below:

F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)=\begin{array}[]{ll}\{e\in E^{i-1}\ |\ \begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{wspec}(e)(y,[\xi^{i-1},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow\text{and}\ (e,e_{i-1}^{x_{1-l}})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1})^{*}\end{array}\}&\text{if }i=d_{v}\\
\{e\in E^{i-1}\ |\ \begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{wspec}(e)(y,[\xi^{i-1},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow\text{and}\ (e,e_{i-1}^{x_{l}})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1})^{*}\end{array}\}&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{array}

Then, we define
\xi^{i}_{-1}
as the abstract execution of the history
h^{i}_{-1}={({E^{i}_{-1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{-1}})}
obtained by appending
e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}}
to
h^{i}_{-1}
and
\xi^{i}_{-1}
as follows:
E^{i}_{-1}
contains
E^{i-1}
and events
e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}}
. First of all, we require that the relations
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{-1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{-1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}_{-1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}_{-1}
contain
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i-1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i-1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i-1}
respectively.

With respect to events
e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}}
, we impose that
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is the maximal event w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{-1}
among those on the same replica. Also,
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is maximal w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
as we define that for every object
z
,
(({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{-1})_{z})^{-1}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})=\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(z,c_{i}^{x_{l}}(z))
. We also require that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}_{-1}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}_{-1}
. With respect to
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}_{-1}
, we impose that
e_{i}^{x_{0}}
succeeds every event in
E^{i}
w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}_{-1}
and that
e_{i}^{x_{1}}
is the maximum event w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
in
\xi^{i}_{-1}
.

We use
\xi^{i}_{-1}
to construct
\xi^{i}
. If event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is not a conditional write event,
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{-1}
. Otherwise, if event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a conditional write event, given
W_{i}^{x_{l}}
and object
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
, we select an execution-corrector for
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
and
a_{i}^{x_{l}}
. W.l.o.g., we assume that every event mapped by
a_{i}^{x_{l}}
happens on replica
r_{i}^{x_{l}}
. Observe that by the choice of sets
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
and
W_{i}^{x_{l}}
, and thanks to the assumptions on storages (see
Section

7.4
), such event(s) are always well-defined.

In addition, we denote by
C_{i}^{x_{l}}
to the set of objects we need to correct for
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
. More specifically, if
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a conditional write-read, we denote by
C_{i}^{x_{l}}
to the set of objects
y
s.t.
a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)
is defined, i.e.
C_{i}^{x_{l}}=\{y\in\mathsf{Objs}\ |\ a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)\downarrow\}
. In the case
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is not a conditional write-read, we use the convention
C_{i}^{x_{l}}=\emptyset
. The set of events in
\xi^{i}
is the following:
E^{i}=E^{i-1}\cup\bigcup_{l\in\{0,1\}}(\{e_{i}^{x_{l}}\}\bigcup_{y\in C_{i}\setminus\{o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\}}a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))
. Observe that by the choice of
C_{i}^{x_{l}}
, the set
E_{i}
is well-defined.

From
\xi^{i}_{-1}
, we define
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{0}\overset{\mathsf{seq}(a_{i})}{\curlyvee}e_{i}
as the corrected execution of
\xi
and
e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}}
with events
a_{i}^{x_{0}},a_{i}^{x_{1}}
. For describing
\xi^{i}
, we consider
<
to be a well-founded order over
\mathsf{Objs}
.
\xi^{i}
satisfies the following:

•

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
: Let
y\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
. We require that for every event
e\in E^{i-1}
,
(e,a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
iff
\mathtt{rep}(e)=r_{i}^{x_{l}},0\leq j<i
. We also require that
(\textup{{{init}}},a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
and
(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y),e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
. Finally, we require that for every objects
y^{\prime}\in C_{i}^{x_{l}},y^{\prime}<y
,
(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y^{\prime}),a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}
: Let
y
be an object in
C_{i}^{x_{l}}
. For every object
z
, if
z\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
and
z<y
, we require that
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{z})^{-1}(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))=\mathsf{rspec}(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))(z,c_{i}^{x_{l}}(z)\oplus a_{i}^{x_{l}}(z))
; while otherwise, we require that
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{z})^{-1}(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))=\mathsf{rspec}(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))(z,c_{i}^{x_{l}}(z))
. We also require that for every object
z
, if
z\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
, then
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{z})^{-1}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})=\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(z,c_{i}^{x_{l}}(z)\oplus a_{i}^{x_{l}}(z))
, while otherwise,
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}_{z})^{-1}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})=\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(z,c_{i}^{x_{l}}(z))
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
: Let
y\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
. We require that for every object
y\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
and event
e
s.t.
(e,a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
, s.t.
(e,a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}
,
(e,a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
: We impose that for every event
e\in E^{i-1}
,
(e,a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i},y\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
. We also require that for every pair of objects
y_{1},y_{2}\in C_{i}
s.t.
y_{1},y_{2}
,
(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y_{1}),a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y_{2}))\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
. As a tie-breaker between events associated to
x_{0}
and
x_{1}
, we require that for every pair of events
e\in\{e_{i}^{x_{0}},a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y)\ |\ y\in C_{i}^{x_{0}}\}
,
e^{\prime}\in\{e_{i}^{x_{1}},a_{i}^{x_{1}}(y)\ |\ y\in C_{i}^{x_{0}}\}
,
(e,e^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}
.

We then define
h^{i}={({E^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}})}
and
\xi^{i}={({h^{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}^{i}})}
. Observe that by construction of
h^{i}
and
\xi^{i}
, as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}
, they satisfy
Definitions

3.2
and
3.4
respectively; so they are a history and an abstract execution respectively. Also, observe that
\xi^{i}
is the corrected abstract execution of
\xi^{i}_{-1}
for events
e_{i}^{x_{0}},e_{i}^{x_{1}}
with events
a_{i}^{x_{0}},a_{i}^{x_{1}}
, i.e.
\xi^{i}=\xi^{i}_{1}=\xi^{i}_{0}\overset{\mathsf{seq}(a_{i}^{x_{1}})}{\curlyvee}e_{i}^{x_{1}}
, where
\xi^{i}_{0}=\xi^{i}_{-1}\overset{\mathsf{seq}(a_{i}^{x_{0}})}{\curlyvee}e_{i}^{x_{0}}
.

Then, we define
\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}=E^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
as the set our program employs. The set
\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
induces the set of traces
\mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{p}}
.

We define the program
P={({S_{\mathsf{p}},A_{\mathsf{p}},s_{0}^{\mathsf{p}},\Delta_{\mathsf{p}}})}
, where
\textup{{{init}}}_{\mathsf{p}}=\textup{{{init}}}
and
\Delta_{\mathsf{p}}
is the transition function defined as follows: for every trace
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{p}}
and event
e\in\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{p}}(t,e)\downarrow
if and only if
e\not\in t
and every event in
\mathsf{Events}_{\mathsf{p}}
whose replica coincide with
e
and has smaller identifier than
e
is included in
t
.

The rest of the proof, which proceeds as follows, essentially combines previous results obtained while proving
Lemma

6.7
and
Theorem

B.9
:

(1)

There exists a finite trace
t
of

\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}}I_{E}
that contains no receive action (
Lemma

C.5
)

(2)

The trace
t
induces a history
h_{\mathsf{v}}={({E,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}})}
and an abstract execution
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
. As
I_{E}
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
,
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
is valid w.r.t.
\mathsf{Spec}
.
Next, we prove that since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
, events in
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
read the latests value w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
for any object. In particular, we deduce that
\xi_{v}
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
(
Corollary

D.5
).

(3)

Since
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
is a total order, either
(e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{0}},e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{1}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
or
(e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{1}},e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{0}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. W.l.o.g., assume that
(e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{0}},e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{1}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. By
Proposition

D.6
, we deduce that
e_{0}^{x_{0}}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x_{0}}(e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}},[\xi_{\mathsf{v}},\mathsf{CMod}])
. The proof is explained in
Figure

9
: if
(e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{0}},e_{d_{\mathsf{v}}-1}^{x_{1}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, then all events
e_{i}^{x_{0}}
form a path in such way that
\mathsf{v}_{x_{0}}(e_{0}^{x_{0}},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}})
holds in
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
. If some event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
is a conditional read-write event, the predicate
\mathsf{conflict}_{x}(e_{0}^{x_{0}},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}})
holds in
\xi_{\mathsf{v}}
thanks to the corrector events
A_{i}^{x_{l}}
.

(4)

As
e_{0}^{x_{0}}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x_{0}}(e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{0}},[\xi_{\mathsf{v}},\mathsf{CMod}])
but
(e_{0}^{x_{0}},e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}})\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
(no message is received), we deduce in
Proposition

B.16
that
\mathsf{OpSpec}
is layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. By contrapositive, if
\mathsf{OpSpec}
would be layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
, as
e_{0}^{x_{0}}\in\mathsf{ctxt}_{x_{0}}(e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}},[\xi_{\mathsf{v}},\mathsf{CMod}])
, there would exist an event
e
s.t.
(e_{0}^{x_{0}},e)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
and
e\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}})(x_{0},[\xi_{\mathsf{v}},\mathsf{CMod}])
. However, as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
,
\mathtt{rep}(e_{0}^{x_{0}})=\mathtt{rep}(e)=\mathtt{rep}(e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}})
which is false because
\mathtt{rep}(e_{0}^{x_{0}})=r_{0}
and
\mathtt{rep}(e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}})=r_{1}
.

(5)

Since
\mathsf{rspec}
is maximally layered, we can show that the layer bound of
\mathsf{rspec}
is smaller than or equal to the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
\mathsf{v}
(
Proposition

B.17
). Observe that an event writes
x_{0}
only if it is
init
or is an event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
s.t.
\varepsilon_{i}\in E_{x}
and
l=0
. Any such index
i
corresponds to a suffix of
\mathsf{v}
. By causal suffix closure, for any arbitration-free suffix
v^{\prime}
of
v
there is a visibility formula that subsumes
v^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
. As
d_{\mathsf{v}}
is the maximum index for which
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, the number of events writing
x_{0}
in replica
r_{1}
distinct from
init
coincide with the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
\mathsf{v}
. Hence, as
\mathsf{rspec}
is layered w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, if its layer bound would be greater than the number of arbitration-free suffixes,
e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}}
would necessarily read
x_{0}
from
init
(other events writing
x_{0}
are in replica
r_{0}
and
e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}
only reads from events in
r_{1}
). However, as
\mathsf{rspec}
is maximally-layered and
e_{0}^{x_{0}}
succeeds
init
w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{+}
, we would conclude that
e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}}
would also read
x_{0}
from
e_{0}^{x_{0}}
. However, this is impossible as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
but
e_{0}^{x_{0}}
is in replica
r_{0}
and
e_{\mathsf{len}(\mathsf{v})}^{x_{0}}
is in replica
r_{1}
.

(6)

Lastly, we show in
Proposition

B.18
that if the layer bound of
\mathsf{rspec}
is smaller than or equal to the number of arbitration-free suffixes of
v
, then
v
is vacuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{OpSpec}
, which contradicts the fact that
\mathsf{v}
is a visibility formula from the normal form
\mathsf{n}\mathsf{CMod}_{\mathsf{OpSpec}}
.
∎

Proposition D.3.

The abstract execution
\xi^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
described in
Lemma

8.2
satisfies that for every
i,0\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v),l\in\{0,1\}
:

(1)

For every object
y\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
, the following conditions hold:

(a)

For every object
z\in\mathsf{Objs}
, if
z\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
and
z<y
,
G(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y),z)=F_{i}^{x_{l}}(z)\cup\{a_{i}^{x_{l}}(z)\}
, while otherwise,
G(a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y),z)=F_{i}^{x_{l}}(z)
.

(b)

The execution
\xi^{i}_{l}\restriction y
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

(2)

For the event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
, the following conditions hold:

(a)

For every object
z
, if
z\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
,
G(e_{i}^{x_{l}},z)=F_{i}^{x_{l}}(z)\cup\{a_{i}^{x_{l}}(z)\}
, while otherwise
G(e_{i}^{x_{l}},z)=F_{i}^{x_{l}}(z)
.

(b)

The execution
\xi^{i}_{l}
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

where
\mathsf{ctxt}_{z}(e,[\xi^{\mathsf{len}(v)},\textup{{CC}}])={({G(e,z),{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}_{\restriction G(e,z)\times G(e,z)},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}_{\restriction G(e,z)\times G(e,z)}})}
.

Proof.

The proof of this result essentially coincides with that of
Proposition

B.12
.

We prove the result by induction. In particular, we show that for every
i,-1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
and object
y
, either (0)
i=-1
or (1) and (2) hold. The base case,
i=-1
, is immediate as (0) holds; so let us suppose that the result holds for every
j,-1\leq j<i
, and let us prove it for
i
.

For proving the inductive step, we first prove (1) for
l=0
, then (2) for
l=0
, and then (1) and (2) for
l=1
. As both (1) and (2) have an identical proof (observe that the role of object
y
in the former is just to declare that event
a_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)
is well-defined and the role of
l
is to determine which session must be proven first), we present only the proof of (1) for
l=0
.

We show (1) by transfinite induction. Let
\alpha
be an ordinal of cardinality
|\mathsf{Objs}|
. For every
k,0\leq k\leq\alpha
, we denote by
V_{k}
to the set containing the first
k
elements in
\mathsf{Objs}
according to
<
. We show that (1) holds for every
y\in V_{k}\cap C_{i}^{x_{0}}
.

The base,
V_{0}
is immediate as
V_{0}=\emptyset
. We thus focus on the successor case (i.e., showing that if (1) holds for every object
y\in V_{k}\cap C_{i}^{x_{0}}
it also holds for
V_{k+1}
), as the limit case is immediate: if
k
is a limit ordinal,
V_{k}=\bigcup_{i,i<k}V_{i}
; so (1) immediately holds. For showing that (1) holds for every object
y\in V_{k+1}\cap C_{i}^{x_{0}}
, as by induction hypothesis it holds for every object
y\in V_{k}\cap C_{i}^{x_{0}}
, it suffices to show it for the only object
y\in V_{k+1}\setminus V_{i}
. W.l.o.g., we can assume that
y\in C_{i}^{x_{0}}
; as otherwise the result is immediate.

We first prove (1a) and then we show (1b). Let
z\in\mathsf{Objs}
be an object. Two cases arise:
z\in C_{i},z<y
or not. Both cases are identical, so we present the former, i.e., if
z\in C_{i}^{x_{0}},z<y
, then
F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\cup\{a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\}=G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
.

For proving that
F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\cup\{a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\}\subseteq G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
, we distinguish whether
i=d_{v}
or not. However, the proof essentially coincides in both cases, so we present the case
i=d_{v}
. We split the proof in two blocks: showing that
F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\subseteq G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
and showing that
a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\in G(a_{i}(y),z)
.

For showing that
F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\subseteq G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
, let
e
be an event in
F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)
. In such case,
e\in E^{i-1}
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,e_{i-1}^{x_{1}})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{*}
. By the construction of
\xi
, it is easy to see that any such event belongs to
E^{i}
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,e_{i-1}^{x_{1}})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)})^{*}
. As
i=d_{v}
, we deduce that
(e_{i-1}^{x_{1}},a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
. Hence,
(e,a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)})^{+}
; so
e\in G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
. This show that
F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\subseteq G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
.

For showing that
a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\in G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
, we observe that
\xi^{i}_{0}=\xi^{i}_{-1}\overset{a_{i}^{x_{0}}}{\curlyvee}e_{i}^{x_{0}}
. We note that as
z<y
, by induction hypothesis (1b),
\xi^{i}_{0}\restriction z
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Thus, by Property
1
of
Definition

7.13
,
\mathsf{wspec}(a_{i}(z))(z,[\xi^{i}_{0},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
. Hence,
\mathsf{wspec}(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z))(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
\mathsf{wspec}(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z))(z,[\xi^{\mathsf{len}(v)},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
. As
z<y
,
(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z),a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y))\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
; so we conclude that
a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\in G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
.

We conclude the proof of the inductive step of (1a) by showing the converse i.e.
F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\cup\{a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)\}\supseteq G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
. Let
e\in G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
. First of all, by the definition of
Causal
visibility formula (see
Figure

4(b)
),
e\in G(a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y),z)
iff
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)})^{+}
. Observe that if
(e,a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)})^{+}
, by construction of
\xi^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
, such event must belong to
E^{i}
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
and
(e,a_{i}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{+}
. We prove that if
e\in E^{i-1}
then
e\in F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)
, while otherwise, if
e\in E^{i}\setminus E^{i-1}
, then
e=a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)
.

If
e\in E^{i-1}
, as
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i-1},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
. Also, as
i=d_{v}
and
(e,a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{+}
, we deduce that
(e,e_{i-1}^{x_{1}})\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i-1})^{*}
. In other words,
e\in F_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)
.

Otherwise, if
e\in E^{i}\setminus E^{i-1}
, we note that by construction of
\xi^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
, the only events in
E^{i}\setminus E^{i-1}
s.t.
(e,a_{i}^{x_{0}}(y))\in({\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{i})^{+}
are events
a_{i}^{x_{0}}(w),w\in C_{i},w<y
. As
\xi^{i}_{0}=\xi^{i}_{-1}\overset{\mathsf{seq}(a_{i}^{x_{0}})}{\curlyvee}e_{i}^{x_{0}}
and
z<y
,
\xi^{i}_{0}\restriction z
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
. Hence,
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
iff
\mathsf{wspec}(e)(z,[\xi^{i}_{0},\textup{{CC}}])\downarrow
. Thus, by Property
1
of
Definition

7.13
we conclude that
e=a_{i}^{x_{0}}(z)
.

For concluding the inductive step, we show that (1b) holds. This is immediate by the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i}
: for every event
e\in\xi^{i}\restriction y
, by induction hypothesis (1a) or (2a) – depending on whether
e=e_{j}^{x_{l^{\prime}}}
or
a_{j}^{x_{l^{\prime}}}(w)
, where
0\leq j\leq i,w\in C_{i},l^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
–
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}^{i})_{z}^{-1}(e)=\mathsf{rspec}(e)(\textup{{CC}},[\xi^{j}_{l^{\prime}}\restriction y,z])=\mathsf{rspec}(e)(\textup{{CC}},[\xi^{i}_{l}\restriction y,z])
. Thus,
\xi^{i}\restriction y
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.
∎

A consequence of
Proposition

D.3
is the following result.

Corollary D.4.

The abstract execution
\xi
described in
Lemma

8.2
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

Corollary

D.5
is an immediate result from
Corollary

D.4
, obtained by simply observing that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
.

Corollary D.5.

The abstract execution
\xi_{v}
described in
Lemma

8.2
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
.

Proposition D.6.

For every
l\in\{0,1\}
, if
(e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{l}},e_{d_{v}-1}^{x_{1-l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
, then the predicate
v_{x_{0}}(e_{0}^{x_{l}},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}})
holds in the abstract execution
\xi={({h,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}},{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}})}
described in
Theorem

B.9
.

Proof.

The proof of this result essentially coincides with that of
Proposition

B.14
.

The proof is a simple consequence of
\xi^{\mathsf{len}(v)}
’s construction. To show that
v_{x_{0}}(e_{0}^{x_{l}},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}})
holds in
\xi
, we first show that for every
i,1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
and to then prove that
\mathsf{wrCons}^{\mathsf{v}}_{x}(e_{0}^{x_{l}},\ldots e_{\mathsf{len}(v)}^{x_{l}})
holds in
\xi
.

We prove that for every
i,1\leq i\leq\mathsf{len}(v)
,
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
. Four cases arise depending on
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}
.

•

\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
: In this case, by construction of events
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}}
, we know that
r_{i}^{x_{l}}=r_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
. Hence,
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}^{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
.

•

\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
: In this case, we first show that there is an object
y\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}\cap W_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\setminus C_{i}^{x_{l}}
, and then show that
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{y}
. For showing the first part, we distinguish between cases depending on whether
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}
or not.

–

o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}
: In this sub-case, we show that
y=o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
. On one hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}=\emptyset
, by the choice of event
e_{i}^{x_{l}}
,
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\setminus C_{i}^{x_{l}}
. On the other hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
, as
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}
, we deduce that
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows multi-object read-write events. Observe that as
v
is conflict-maximal,
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i-1)}\neq\emptyset
. Hence, as
\mathsf{OpSpec}
allows multi-object read-write events, we deduce that
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\setminus C_{i}^{x_{l}}
. In both cases, as
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i-1)}\neq\emptyset
and
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
, by the choice of
W_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
, we conclude that
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in W_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
.

–

o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\not\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}
: In this case, we show that
y=\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
. On one hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}=\emptyset
,
X_{i}^{x_{l}}=\emptyset
; so by the choice of
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}
(see
Equation

65
),
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}=\tilde{x}_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
. By the choice of
D_{i}^{x_{l}}
,
\tilde{x}_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}\setminus C_{i}^{x_{l}}
. Moreover, as
v
is conflict-maximal,
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i-1)}\neq\emptyset
; so
\tilde{x}_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in X_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
. By the choice of event
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
,
X_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\subseteq W_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
. Altogether, we conclude that
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}\in W_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
.

On the other hand, if
\mathsf{conflictsOf(v,i)}\neq\emptyset
, we note that
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}\setminus C_{i}^{x_{l}}
. As
o_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\not\in D_{i}^{x_{l}}
, we deduce that
\mathsf{OpSpec}
only allows single-object read-write events. Thus,
D_{i}^{x_{l}}=\{\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}\}
. As
v
is conflict-maximal, we deduce that
X_{i}^{x_{l}}\subseteq X_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
. As by the choice of
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
,
X_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\subseteq W_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
, we conclude that
\tilde{x}_{i}^{x_{l}}\in W_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
.

We prove now that
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{y}
. First, we show that
{e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}}\ \mathsf{writes}\ {y}\ \mathsf{in}\ {\xi}
. On one hand, if
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
is an unconditional write event,
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}})(y,c_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\downarrow
. On the other hand, if
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
is a conditional write event, as
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
(\textup{{CC}},\mathsf{OpSpec})
(
Corollary

D.5
) and
y\in W_{i}^{x_{l}}
, by Property
2
of
Definition

7.13
, we deduce that
\mathsf{wspec}(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}})(y,c_{i}^{x_{l}}(y))\downarrow
. Then, as
\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}
,
i\neq d_{v}
, so
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)
. Observe that by construction of
\xi
,
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
is the
so
-maximum event in
c_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)
. As every event in
F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)
is
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
-related, we deduce that
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
is the
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
-maximum event in
F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)
. We note that as
y\not\in C_{i}^{x_{l}}
, by
Proposition

D.3
,
F_{i}^{x_{l}}(y)=G(e_{i}^{x_{l}},y)
. Altogether,
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
is the
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
-maximum event in
\mathsf{ctxt}_{y}(e_{i}^{x_{l}},[\xi^{\mathsf{len}(v)},\textup{{CC}}])
. As
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}^{\mathsf{len}(v)}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
, we conclude that
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}
is the
{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
-maximum event in
\mathsf{ctxt}_{y}(e_{i}^{x_{l}},[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])
. As
\mathsf{rspec}
is maximally layered, we deduce that
e_{i-1}^{x_{l}}\in\mathsf{rspec}(e_{i}^{x_{l}})(y,[\xi,\textup{{CC}}])
. Finally, as
\xi
is valid w.r.t.
CC
(
Corollary

D.5
), we conclude that
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{wr}}_{y}
.

•

\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
: In this case,
i\neq d_{v}
. Then,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
and
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
, we conclude that
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.1484,1}\mathsf{rb}}
.

•

\mathsf{Rel}_{i}^{\mathsf{v}}={\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
: On one hand, if
i=d_{v}
, by hypothesis,
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.7031,0.4453,0}\mathsf{ar}}
. On the other hand, if
i\neq d_{v}
,
(e_{i-1}^{x_{l}},e_{i}^{x_{l}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{so}}
. Thus,
